\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}2\pi}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\int_{0}^{t-s}h(s+\tau){\mathrm{d}}L_{\tau}\right]{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}2\pi}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{0}^{t-s}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)}{4\pi}h(s+\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{s}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau-s)h(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}s.

We have obtained (
2.6
) for
z^{0}\neq 0
, as required.
The proof of (
2.6
) is complete.

(4
∘
)
Identity (
2.7
) follows immediately from (
1.17
) and (
1.10
) since by (
1.19
),
\{\mathscr{Z}_{t}\}
is a linear transformation of
\{Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\}
and the independent Brownian motions
\{W^{\mathbf{i}\prime}_{t}\}\cup\{W^{k}_{t}\}_{k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\setminus\mathbf{i}}
.

The proof of (
2.8
) uses the same property that
\{\mathscr{Z}_{t}\}
is a linear transformation of
\{Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\}
and
\{W^{\mathbf{i}\prime}_{t}\}\cup\{W^{k}_{t}\}_{k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\setminus\mathbf{i}}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show the following identity using an
M
-dimensional Brownian motion
\{\mathcal{W}_{t}\}
,
M\geq 1
, with transition densities
\{\tilde{p}_{t}(\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})\}
and independent of
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
: For all
f\in\mathscr{B}_{+}(\mathbb{C})
,
\tilde{f}\in\mathscr{B}_{+}({\mathbb{R}}^{M})
,
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and
\tilde{z}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta t}f(Z_{t})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t})}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)};t\geq T_{0}(Z)\right]={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta r}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{r}}[f(Z_{t-r})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-r})]{\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\right].

(2.50)

Here,
\{Z_{t}\}
and
\{\mathcal{W}_{t}\}
have initial conditions
z^{0}
and
\tilde{z}^{0}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}
, and
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}
is similarly defined by extending
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}
under which
\{Z_{t}\}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion. Given the validity of (
2.50
), (
2.8
) follows from the monotone class theorem.

To prove (
2.50
), we handle the two cases
z^{0}=0
and
z^{0}\neq 0
separately. For the case of
z^{0}=0
, first, recall the notation
[\cdot]_{\times}
and
[\cdot]_{\otimes}
defined in and below (
1.24
). Also, observe that by using the inverse local time
\{\tau_{\ell}\}
of
\{L_{r}\}
, for any nonnegative
H
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}H(\mathcal{W}_{r},r){\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{\ell}\leq t\}}H(\mathcal{W}_{\tau_{\ell}},\tau_{\ell}){\mathrm{d}}\ell\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{\ell}\leq t\}}\tilde{p}_{\tau_{\ell}}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})H(\tilde{z}^{1},\tau_{\ell}){\mathrm{d}}\ell\right]{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{p}_{r}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})H(\tilde{z}^{1},r){\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta r}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(r)}{4\pi}\\
\vskip-11.38092pt\\
\tilde{p}_{r}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}H(\tilde{z}^{1},r){\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}r,

(2.51)

where the first and third equalities use the change of variables formula of general Stieltjes integrals
[
34
, (4.9) Proposition on p.8]
, the second equality uses the independence between
\{Z_{t}\}
and
\{\mathcal{W}_{t}\}
,
and the last equality uses (
2.6
) with
z^{0}=0
. Note that the notation
[\cdot]_{\times}
in (
2.51
) is defined in (
1.24
).

The proof of (
2.50
) in the case of
z^{0}=0
now proceeds as follows. By (
2.5
) for
g\equiv 1
and
z^{0}=0
, we can write

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta t}f(Z_{t})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t})}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{C}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(r)}{2\pi}P_{t-r}(z)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta t}f(z)}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)\\
\vskip-11.38092pt\\
\tilde{p}_{t}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{2})\tilde{f}(\tilde{z}^{2})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z\\
{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{2}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}\int_{\mathbb{C}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(r)}{2\pi}P_{t-r}(z)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta t}f(z)}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)\\
\vskip-11.38092pt\\
\tilde{p}_{r}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})\tilde{p}_{t-r}(\tilde{z}^{1},\tilde{z}^{2})\tilde{f}(\tilde{z}^{2})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z\\
{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{2}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}_{1}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta r}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(r)}{4\pi}\\
\vskip-11.38092pt\\
\tilde{p}_{r}(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta r}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0,\tilde{z}^{1}}[f(Z_{t-r})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-r})]{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\tilde{z}^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta r}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{r}}[f(Z_{t-r})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-r})]{\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\right],

(2.52)

where the second equality uses the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation and the last equality uses (
2.51
). By (
2.52
) and the fact that
T_{0}(Z)=0
under
Z_{0}=0
, we have proved (
2.50
) for the case of
z^{0}=0
.

The proof of (
2.50
) in the case of
z^{0}\neq 0
uses the strong Markov property of
\{(Z_{t},\mathcal{W}_{t})\}
at time
T_{0}(Z)
, which holds by the independence between
\{Z_{t}\}
and
\{\mathcal{W}_{t}\}
. Specifically, we obtain the following equalities where the first and third ones use the strong Markov property of
\{(Z_{t},\mathcal{W}_{t})\}
at time
T_{0}(Z)
and the second equality uses (
2.52
) with
t
replaced by
t-s
:

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\Biggl[\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta t}f(Z_{t})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t})}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)};t\geq T_{0}(Z)\Biggr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\Biggl[{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta T_{0}(Z)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}(Z)}}\Biggl[\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta(t-s)}f(Z_{t-s})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-s})}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-s}|)}\Biggr]\Bigg|_{T_{0}(Z)=s};t\geq T_{0}(Z)\Biggr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\Biggl[{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta T_{0}(Z)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}(Z)}}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{t-s}\!\!\!{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta r}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{r}}[f(Z_{t-s-r})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-s-r})]{\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\Biggr]\Bigg|_{T_{0}(Z)=s}\!\!\!;t\geq T_{0}(Z)\Biggr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0}}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{\beta r}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0,\mathcal{W}_{r}}[f(Z_{t-r})\tilde{f}(\mathcal{W}_{t-r})]{\mathrm{d}}L_{r}\Biggr].

The last equality proves (
2.50
) in the case of
z^{0}\neq 0
. The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

3
Recurrence

Our goal in this section is to prove Theorem
3.1
on the recurrence of
\{(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
, where
\{W^{\prime}_{t}\}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion independent of
\{Z_{t}\}
.

Theorem 3.1.

Fix
\sigma\in[0,\infty),\varsigma\in(0,\infty)
, and
\beta,\beta_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. Write
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}
for
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}
under which
W^{\prime}_{0}=z^{1}
, for any
z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}
. Then for all
(z^{0},z^{1})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
, we have the following properties.

(1
∘
)

The
\mathbb{C}^{2}
-valued regular Feller process
\{(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\}
is Harris recurrent and reversible with respect to the following invariant measure, where
\mu_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
is defined by (
2.3
):

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{\beta{\downarrow}}({\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0},{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1})\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\;\mu_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}({\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0}){\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}=\frac{2\beta}{\pi}K(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1},\quad\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1}\in\mathbb{C}.

(3.1)

(2
∘
)

For the Markovian local time
\{L_{t}\}
of
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|){\mathrm{d}}L_{t}=\infty\quad{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}\mbox{-a.s. }

(3.2)

(3
∘
)

For all
1<q_{0}<1+1/{\sqrt{2}}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{q\searrow 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma Z_{t}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]{\mathrm{d}}t<\infty.

(3.3)

See Section
3.1
for the proofs of Theorem
3.1
(1
∘
) and (2
∘
) and Section
3.2
for the proof of Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
).

Theorem
3.1
shows the recurrence of
\{(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\}
in different forms.
First, for
Theorem
3.1
(1
∘
), the regular Feller property and the invariance of
\mathbf{m}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
are immediate by Theorem
2.1
(1
∘
) and the fact that two-dimensional Brownian motion is a regular Feller process invariant and reversible with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{C}
. (Recall the discussion below Theorem
2.1
for the definition of a Feller process being
regular
.) Then the
Harris recurrence
of
\{(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\}
refers to the following property:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\Gamma}(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}t=\infty\quad{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}\mbox{-a.s.},\quad\forall\;(z^{0},z^{1})\in\mathbb{C}^{2},\;\Gamma\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{C}^{2})\mbox{ with }\mathbf{m}^{\beta{\downarrow}}(\Gamma)>0,

(3.4)

which specializes a general definition from
[
24
, p.400]
. Also, Theorem
3.1
(2
∘
) is a corollary of Theorem
3.1
(1
∘
), and Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
) serves as a technical property applied together with Theorem
3.1
(2
∘
) in
[
12
]
. We regard Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
) as an integrated form of recurrence as we combine the weak convergence of
\mathds{1}_{t>0}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathrm{d}}t
to
\delta_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}t)
as
q\searrow 0
and the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\searrow 0
and
x\nearrow\infty
.

3.1
Harris recurrence

In this subsection, we give the proofs of Theorem
3.1
(1
∘
) and (2
∘
).

Proof of Theorem
3.1
(1
∘
).

By a theorem ensuring the Harris recurrence of a general regular Feller process
[
24
, Theorem 20.17, pp.405–406]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,0}((Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\in\Gamma){\mathrm{d}}t=\infty

(3.5)

for any compact set
\Gamma
with a nonzero Lebesgue measure is sufficient to get the required Harris recurrence. To prove this property, first, recall Theorem
2.1
(2
∘
), the definition (
1.8
) of
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
,
and the notation of multiplication columns
[\cdot]_{\times}
defined in (
1.24
) and its analogue
[\cdot]_{\otimes}
for measures. Then by the assumed independence of
\{Z_{t}\}
and
\{W_{t}^{\prime}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
,

\displaystyle\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,0}((Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\in\Gamma){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{2\pi}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)P_{t-\tau}(z_{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)\\
P_{t}(z^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\mathds{1}_{\Gamma}(z^{0},z^{1})\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta(t-\tau)}}{2\pi}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)P_{t-\tau}(z^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)\\
P_{\tau}(w)P_{t-\tau}(w,z^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}

\displaystyle\quad\;\times\mathds{1}_{\Gamma}(z^{0},z^{1})\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}w

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
P_{\tau}(w)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t^{\prime}}(z^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)\\
P_{t^{\prime}}(w,z^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\\
&\quad\;\times\mathds{1}_{\Gamma}(z^{0},z^{1})\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}w.\end{split}

(3.6)

Here, we write
P_{t}(z^{1})
as
\int P_{\tau}(w)P_{t-\tau}(w,z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}w

by the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation and change the order of integration from
{\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}t
to
{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\tau
in the second equality, and the last equality uses the change of variables
t^{\prime}=t-\tau
. Note that the notations
[\cdot]_{\times}
and
[\cdot]_{\otimes}
are defined in and below (
1.24
).

Let us make some observations for the right-hand side of (
3.6
). First, note that the function

w\mapsto\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t^{\prime}}(z^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)\\
P_{t^{\prime}}(w,z^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\mathds{1}_{\Gamma}(z^{0},z^{1})\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime},\quad w\in\mathbb{C},

is bounded continuous, and since the Lebesgue measure of
\Gamma
is positive by assumption, the function takes values in
(0,\infty)
. Therefore, with
B(0,R)
denoting the open ball in
\mathbb{C}
centered at
0
with radius
R
, (
3.6
) implies that for any
R\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0,0}((Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\in\Gamma){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\geq C(R,\Gamma,\beta)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
P_{\tau}(0,B(0,R))\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\geq C(R,\Gamma,\beta)\int_{1/\beta}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
1/\tau\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}\tau,

(3.7)

since

2\pi\tau P_{\tau}(0,B(0,R))=\int_{|z^{1}|\leq R}{\mathrm{e}}^{-|z^{1}|^{2}/(2\tau)}{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\to\int_{|z^{1}|\leq R}{\mathrm{d}}z^{1}
as
\tau\to\infty
. To use (
3.7
), we recall the definition (
1.8
) of
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
and write

\displaystyle\int_{1/\beta}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
1/\tau\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{1/\beta}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\beta^{u}\tau^{u-2}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}u{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\beta\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}u=\infty,

(3.8)

where the second equality uses the change of variables
t=\beta\tau
, and the last equality holds since
\int_{0}^{1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq 1
for all
u\geq 2
and
\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}{\mathrm{d}}t=\Gamma(u-1)\to\infty
as
u\to\infty
so that the following asymptotic representation as
u\to\infty
holds:

\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{\int_{1}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}{\mathrm{d}}t}{u\Gamma(u-1)}\sim\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}t^{u-2}{\mathrm{d}}t}{u\Gamma(u-1)}=\frac{1}{u}.

By (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), we obtain (
3.5
). The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
3.1
(2
∘
).

To prove (
3.2
), recall that
we have explained below Theorem
3.1
why
\mathbf{m}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
is invariant for
\{(Z_{t},W^{\prime}_{t})\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
.
Since

\displaystyle g\mapsto\sup_{T>0}\frac{1}{T}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{(\tilde{z}^{0},\tilde{z}^{1})}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}g(Z_{s},W^{\prime}_{s})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\varsigma W^{\prime}_{s}|){\mathrm{d}}L_{s}\right]\mathbf{m}^{\beta{\downarrow}}({\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0},{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1})

(3.9)

is a nonzero Revuz measure, a general theorem guaranteeing the a.s. explosion of nonnegative additive functionals
[
34
, Proposition 3.11, p.426]
gives (
3.2
); see also
[
34
, p.409]
. Specifically, to apply that general theorem in
[
34
]
, note that the above Harris recurrence implies the Harris recurrence defined in
[
34
, p.425]
by
[
24
, Theorems 20.10, 20.11, and 20.12, pp.397–400]
.

\blacksquare

3.2
The integrated form of recurrence

This subsection gives the proof of Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
). To write out the expectation in (
3.3
),
we use again Theorem
2.1
(2
∘
) for
g\equiv 1
, the definition (
1.8
) of
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
and the assumed independence
\{Z_{t}\}{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\{W_{t}^{\prime}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
. Also, we use the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation to rewrite the transition density
P_{t}(z^{1},\tilde{z}^{1})
for
W^{\prime}_{t}
. Hence, for
z^{0}\neq 0
and all
z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma Z_{t}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t}(z^{0},\tilde{z}^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)\\
P_{t}(z^{1},\tilde{z}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma\tilde{z}^{0}+\varsigma\tilde{z}^{1}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{2\pi{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\begin{bmatrix}P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})\\
P_{s}(z^{1},w)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}

\displaystyle\quad\;\times{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(0,w)}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}s}|\sigma Z_{t-s}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t-s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-s}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]{\mathrm{d}}w{\mathrm{d}}s,

and

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(0,z^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma Z_{t}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{3}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
P_{\tau}(z^{1},w)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(\tilde{z}^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)\\
P_{t-\tau}(w,\tilde{z}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma\tilde{z}^{0}+\varsigma\tilde{z}^{1}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathrm{d}}w\otimes\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}\tau.

Next, to compute the Laplace transforms of the right-hand sides, we write

\displaystyle U^{q_{0}}_{q}(w^{0},w^{1})

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t}(w^{0},\tilde{z}^{0})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)\\
P_{t}(w^{1},\tilde{z}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}

\displaystyle\quad\;\times\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma\tilde{z}^{0}+\varsigma\tilde{z}^{1}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|\tilde{z}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{0}\\
{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{z}^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathbb{E}}^{(W^{0},W^{1})}_{(w^{0},w^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma W^{0}_{t}+\varsigma W_{t}^{1}|}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W_{t}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|)\right]{\mathrm{d}}t,

(3.10)

where
W^{0}
and
W^{1}
are independent two-dimensional standard Brownian motions.
Since the Laplace transform of a convolution gives a product of Laplace transforms and
P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})=2^{-1}P_{s}(z^{0})
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\;\int_{0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(z^{0},z^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma Z_{t}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]{\mathrm{d}}t\\
&=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{qU^{q_{0}}_{q+\beta}(z^{0},z^{1})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}+\frac{2\pi}{2K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-(q+\beta)t}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t}(z^{0})\\
P_{t}(z^{1},w)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\vskip-5.69046pt\\
\quad\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{(0,w)}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma Z_{t}+\varsigma W^{\prime}_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}\right]{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}w,&z^{0}\neq 0,\\
\vskip-5.69046pt\\
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{q}{2\pi}{\mathrm{e}}^{-(q+\beta)t}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t)\\
P_{t}(z^{1},w)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}tU^{q_{0}}_{q+\beta}(0,w){\mathrm{d}}w,&z^{0}=0.\end{cases}\end{aligned}

(3.11)

Our goal is to bound the limit superior of the right-hand side of (
3.11
) as
q\searrow 0
. To this end, we first prove the following lemma. It gives a choice of
q_{0}>0
for bounding
U^{q_{0}}_{\beta}=\lim_{q\searrow 0}{\uparrow}U^{q_{0}}_{q+\beta}
.

Lemma 3.2.

For all
w^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and
1<q_{0}<1+1/{\sqrt{2}}
,
U^{q_{0}}_{\beta}
defined by (
3.10
) satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{w^{1}\in\mathbb{C}}U^{q_{0}}_{\beta}(w^{0},w^{1})<\infty.

(3.12)

Proof.
We consider the following for a pair of Hölder conjugates
(p_{1},q_{1})
such that
1<q_{1}<\infty
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(W^{0},W^{1})}_{(w^{0},w^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma W^{0}_{t}+\varsigma W_{t}^{1}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W_{t}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|)\right]\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{(W^{0},W^{1})}_{(w^{0},w^{1})}[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma W^{0}_{t}+\varsigma W_{t}^{1}|)^{q_{0}p_{1}}]^{1/p_{1}}\\
&\quad\;\times{\mathbb{E}}^{W^{0}}_{w^{0}}[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|)^{(1-q_{0})q_{1}}]^{1/q_{1}}.\end{split}

(3.13)

To bound the first multiplicative factor on the right-hand side of (
3.13
), we use the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) of
K_{0}
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
to the effect of

K_{0}(x)^{q_{0}p_{1}}\leq C(q_{0},q_{1},\gamma)x^{-p_{1}\gamma},\quad\forall\;x,\gamma\in(0,\infty).

Also, the following bounds for a Bessel process
\{\rho_{t}\}
of index
0
hold: for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,2)
,

\sup_{x_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{0}}^{\rho}[\rho_{t}^{-\gamma^{\prime}}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{0}^{\rho}[\rho_{t}^{-\gamma^{\prime}}]={\mathbb{E}}^{\rho}_{0}[\rho_{1}^{-\gamma^{\prime}}]t^{-\gamma^{\prime}/2}<\infty,\quad\forall\;t\in(0,\infty);

see
[
11
, Lemma 6.2]
for details. Then with a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{W^{\prime}_{t}\}
, applying the last two displays in the same order gives, for all
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
such that
p_{1}\gamma\in(0,2)
,

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(W^{0},W^{1})}_{(w^{0},w^{1})}[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma W^{0}_{t}+\varsigma W_{t}^{1}|)^{q_{0}p_{1}}]^{1/p_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta_{0},q_{0},q_{1},\gamma,\sigma,\varsigma){\mathbb{E}}^{W^{\prime}}_{0}\left[|W^{\prime}_{t}|^{-p_{1}\gamma}\right]^{1/p_{1}}

\displaystyle=C(\beta_{0},q_{0},q_{1},\gamma,\sigma,\varsigma)t^{-\gamma/2},\quad\forall\;t\in(0,\infty).

(3.14)

To bound the second multiplicative factor on the right-hand side of (
3.13
), note that since
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
implies

{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathrm{e}}^{a|Z|}]\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathrm{e}}^{aZ}]=2{\mathrm{e}}^{a^{2}/2}
for all
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{W^{1}}_{0}[{\mathrm{e}}^{\lambda|W^{0}_{1}|}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{W^{1}}_{0}[{\mathrm{e}}^{\lambda|{\rm Re}W^{0}_{1}|+\lambda|{\rm Im}W^{0}_{1}|}]\leq 4{\mathrm{e}}^{\lambda^{2}},\quad\forall\;\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(3.15)

Also, recall that
1<q_{0}<1+1/{\sqrt{2}}
, and note that
the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) of
K_{0}
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
imply that for any
\eta>0
,
K_{0}(x)^{-1}\leq C(\eta){\mathrm{e}}^{(1+\eta)x}
for all
x\geq 0
. Applying this bound on
K_{0}^{-1}(x)
and (
3.15
) in the same order gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{W^{0}}_{w^{0}}[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|)^{(1-q_{0})q_{1}}]^{1/q_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(q_{0},q_{1},\eta){\mathbb{E}}^{W^{0}}_{w^{0}}\bigl[{\mathrm{e}}^{(1+\eta)(q_{0}-1)q_{1}\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|}\bigr]^{1/q_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,q_{0},q_{1},\eta,w_{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{(1+\eta)^{2}(q_{0}-1)^{2}q_{1}\cdot 2\beta t},\quad\forall\;t\in(0,\infty).

(3.16)

Recall that
(p_{1},q_{1})
used above is a pair of Hölder conjugates.
Applying (
3.14
) and (
3.16
) gives the following under the condition of
1<q_{1}<\infty
,
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
with
p_{1}\gamma\in(0,2)
, and
\eta>0
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\;\sup_{w^{1}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(W^{0},W^{1})}_{(w^{0},w^{1})}\left[\left(\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{0}}|\sigma W^{0}_{t}+\varsigma W_{t}^{1}|}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W_{t}^{0}|)}\right)^{q_{0}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|W^{0}_{t}|)\right]\\
&\leq C(\beta,\beta_{0},q_{0},q_{1},\gamma,\sigma,\varsigma,\eta,w_{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{(1+\eta)^{2}(q_{0}-1)^{2}q_{1}\cdot 2\beta t}t^{-\gamma/2},\quad\forall\;t\in(0,\infty).\end{split}

(3.17)

On the other hand, we can choose
1<q_{1}<\infty
and
\eta>0
such that
(1+\eta)^{2}(q_{0}-1)^{2}q_{1}\cdot 2<1
since
1<q_{0}<1+1/{\sqrt{2}}
.
With respect to this
q_{1}
, we validate (
3.17
) by choosing
small enough
\gamma\in(0,2)
such that
p_{1}\gamma\in(0,2)
, so (
3.12
) follows. The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

End of the proof of Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
).

For
z^{0}=0
, we pass the
q\searrow 0
limit superior of the corresponding term on the right-hand side of (
3.11
) and use
Lemma
3.2
and the following limit:

\int_{0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-(q+\beta)t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t){\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{4\pi q}{\log[(q+\beta)/\beta]}\to 4\pi\beta,\quad q\searrow 0,

where the first equality can be seen by an inspection of (
2.49
) (or just use
[
10
, Proposition 5.1, p.176]
), and
the limit holds since
\lim_{x\searrow 0}x^{-1}\log(x+1)=1
.
Moreover, this argument shows that the limit superiors in (
3.3
) for
z^{0}=0
and
z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}
are uniformly bounded. Hence, (
3.3
) for
z^{0}=0
extends to (
3.3
) for
z^{0}\neq 0
by passing the limit superior of the term for
z^{0}\neq 0
on the right-hand side of (
3.11
) and using Lemma
3.2
again.
We have proved Theorem
3.1
(3
∘
).

\blacksquare

4
SDEs with singular drift

The following theorem extends Theorem
1.1
(3
∘
)
for the purpose of applications in
[
12
,
13
]
. The main tool of the proof is Proposition
4.2
, which will be stated afterward.

Theorem 4.1.

For
\beta_{\mathbf{i}}\in(0,\infty)
and
z_{0}=(z_{0}^{1},\cdots,z_{0}^{N})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\it N}
,
the following holds under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta_{\mathbf{i}}{\downarrow},\mathbf{i}}_{z_{0}}
:

(1
∘
)
The stochastic one-
\delta
motion
\{\mathscr{Z}_{t}\}
defined by (
1.19
)
obeys the following SDEs:

\displaystyle Z^{j}_{t}=z_{0}^{j}-\frac{(\mathds{1}_{j=i\prime}-\mathds{1}_{j=i})}{{\sqrt{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggl(\frac{1}{\overline{Z}^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s+W^{j}_{t},\quad 1\leq j\leq N.

(4.1)

Here,
\widehat{K}_{\nu}(x)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,x^{\nu}K_{\nu}(x)
, where
K_{\nu}(\cdot)
is the Macdonald function of index
\nu
, and with the driving Brownian motion
\{W^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\}
of the SDE of
\{Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\}
obtained in Proposition
4.2
(4
∘
),

\displaystyle W^{i\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{W^{\mathbf{i}\prime}_{t}+W^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}},\quad W^{i}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{W^{\mathbf{i}\prime}_{t}-W^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}}

(4.2)

so that
\{W^{j}_{t}\}_{1\leq j\leq N}
defines a
2N
-dimensional standard Brownian motion.

(2
∘
)
For any
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
, the stochastic relative motion
\{Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\}
from (
1.23
) satisfies the following:

\displaystyle Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\,

\displaystyle=Z_{0}^{\mathbf{j}}-\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{i})}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggl(\frac{1}{\overline{Z}^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s+W^{\mathbf{j}}_{t},

(4.3)

\displaystyle|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle=|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[2-\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{i}){\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}}\biggr)\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}2|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|{\mathrm{d}}B^{\mathbf{j}}_{s},

(4.4)

\displaystyle|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|

\displaystyle=|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|+\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[\frac{1}{2|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}-\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{i})}{2|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}}\biggr)\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s+B^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}.

(4.5)

Here,
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|<\infty
, and the following notation is used:

•

X^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,{\rm Re}(Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t})
and
Y^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,{\rm Im}(Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{t})
.

•

For any
\mathbf{k}=(k\prime,k)\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
,
\sigma(\mathbf{k})\in\{-1,0,1\}^{N}
denotes the column vector such that the
k\prime
-th component is
1
, the
k
-th component is
-1
, and the remaining components are zero.

•

\{W^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion defined by, with real
U^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}
and
V^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}
,

W_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}=U^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{W^{j\prime}_{t}-W^{j}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}}=[W_{t}^{1},\cdots,W_{t}^{N}]^{\top}\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})}{{\sqrt{2}}}

•

\{B^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\}
is a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion defined

\displaystyle B^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{X^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+Y^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}.

(4.6)

(3
∘
)
For
\{U_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
,
\{V_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
and
\{B_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
from
(2
∘
)
, the covariations satisfy the following equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle}U^{\mathbf{j}},U^{\mathbf{k}}{\rangle}_{t}&={\langle}V^{\mathbf{j}},V^{\mathbf{k}}{\rangle}_{t}=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{k})}{2}t,\\
{\langle}U^{\mathbf{j}},V^{\mathbf{k}}{\rangle}_{t}&=0,\end{split}

(4.7)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle}B^{\mathbf{j}},B^{\mathbf{k}}{\rangle}_{t}&=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{k})}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{X^{\mathbf{j}}X^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}+Y^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}Y^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}||Z^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|}{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{k})}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\cos[\arg(Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s})-\arg(Z^{\mathbf{k}}_{s})]{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{k})}{2}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{Z^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr)\frac{|Z^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|}{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}{\mathrm{d}}s.\end{split}

(4.8)

Proof.
For (1
∘
), (
4.1
) follows immediately from Proposition
4.2
(4
∘
) and (
1.19
).
The asserted property of
\{W^{j}_{t}\}_{1\leq j\leq N}
can be justified by
Lévy’s characterization of Brownian motion
[
25
, 3.16 Theorem, p.157]
since
\{W^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\}
,
\{W^{\mathbf{i}\prime}_{t}\}
and
\{W^{k}_{t}\}_{k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\setminus\mathbf{i}}
are independent.

For the properties stated in (2
∘
), first, note that (
4.3
) can be obtained by considering the following terms in the first two cases of SDEs from (
4.1
):

\mp\frac{1}{{\sqrt{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggl(\frac{1}{\overline{Z}^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s=\mp\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}\biggl(\frac{1}{|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|^{2}}\biggr)(Z^{i\prime}_{s}-Z^{i}_{s}){\mathrm{d}}s.

Accordingly, we deduce (
4.3
) after some algebra; see
[
14
, Proposition 2.2]
for details. To obtain (
4.4
), (
4.5
) and the property
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|<\infty
, we use (
4.3
), (
4.6
), and Lemma
5.5
with the choice of

A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{i}}_{t}\equiv-\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{j})\cdot\sigma(\mathbf{i})}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta_{\mathbf{i}}}|Z^{\mathbf{i}}_{s}|)}{\mathrm{d}}s,\quad A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{t}\equiv 0,\quad\forall\;\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{E}_{N},\;\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}\setminus\{\mathbf{i}\},

and
\tau=T
, for any fixed
0<T<\infty
.
Also, the required Brownian property of
\{W^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\}
is straightforward, and
Lévy’s characterization of Brownian motion is enough to give the required Brownian property of
\{B^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}\}
since
\int_{0}^{t}\mathds{1}\{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|=0\}{\mathrm{d}}s=0
by
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|<\infty
.

For (3
∘
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) follow immediately from the definitions of
\{U_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
,
\{V_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
and
\{B_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\}
.

\blacksquare

The main objective of the remaining of Section
4
is to prove the following proposition on
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
. Recall Section
1.2
for the definition of this process.

Proposition 4.2.

(1
∘
)
For all
0<t<\infty
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|Z_{t}|\leq 1\}}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)^{4}|Z_{t}|^{2}}\biggr]<\infty,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\exp\biggl\{\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|Z_{s}|\leq 1\}}{\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}|Z_{s}|^{2}}\biggr\}\biggr]<\infty,\quad\forall\;\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(4.10)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)|Z_{t}|}\biggr]<\infty,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|){\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)|Z_{s}|}\biggr)^{p}\biggr]<\infty,\quad\forall\;1\leq p<\infty.

(4.12)

(2
∘
)
For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, the function

t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{t})]
is continuous in
(0,\infty)
,
where, by identifying
\mathbb{C}
with
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and using the usual inner product
{\langle}\cdot,\cdot{\rangle}
for
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}f(z^{1})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{\Delta f}{2}(z^{1})-\biggl\langle\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)\overline{z}^{1}},\nabla f(z^{1})\biggr\rangle,\quad z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\},

(4.13)

with
\Delta
denoting the two-dimensional Laplacian and
\nabla
denoting the gradient operator.

(3
∘
)
For all
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})]=f(z^{0})+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})]{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.14)

(4
∘
)
Fix
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
. Under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle Z_{t}=z^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{Z}_{s}}\right){\mathrm{d}}s+W_{t}

(4.15)

for a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{W_{t}\}
starting from
0
.

Remark 4.3.

(1
∘
) (
4.13
) is equivalent to (
1.18
) since the latter is for
\{\sqrt{2}Z_{t}\}
.

(2
∘
)
By Itô’s formula, (
1.10
) and (
1.12
) are enough to show that

Z_{t}
,
t<T_{0}(Z)
, satisfies (
4.15
). See Proposition
5.3
(1
∘
) with the setting of Example
5.2
(2
∘
) and
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
. The purpose of (
4.15
) is therefore to extend (
4.15
) to and beyond
T_{0}(Z)
.

(3
∘
) It is not clear to us that (
4.14
) can be obtained from direct functional differentiation of semigroups as in the usual derivation of Kolmogorov’s forward equations. More specifically, differentiating formally the right-hand side of (
2.5
) for
z^{0}=0
and
g\equiv 1
with respect to
t
and applying the Leibniz integral rule to the derivative of the integral term give

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0}[f(Z_{t})]=-\beta{\mathbb{E}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[f(Z_{t})]+\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{2\pi}\biggl({\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t)P_{0}f_{\beta}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}P_{t-\tau}f_{\beta}(0){\mathrm{d}}\tau\biggr),

but the boundary term
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t)P_{0}f_{\beta}(0)
explodes
since
\tau\mapsto P_{\tau}f_{\beta}(0)
has a logarithmic singularity at
\tau=0
by the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
. The same issue applies the case of
z^{0}\neq 0
.
∎

Outline of the proofs of Proposition
4.2
(1
∘
)–(3
∘
).

For (1
∘
), the proof of (
4.9
) is obtained by using the explicit formulas of the probability densities of
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
[Theorem
2.1
(1
∘
)]. The proof of (
4.10
) uses a Grönwall inequality-type argument together with a refinement of (
4.9
). See Section
4.1
for details of the proofs of (
4.9
) and (
4.10
).

To see why (
4.10
) implies (
4.12
), note that by the asymptotic representations (
1.25
), (
1.26
), (
1.27
) and (
1.28
) of
K_{0}(x)
and
K_{1}(x)
as
x\searrow 0
and as
x\nearrow\infty
,
z\mapsto\widehat{K}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)/[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)|z|]
, for
|z|\geq 1
, is bounded. Hence, by the elementary inequality
(a+b)^{p}\leq C(p)(a^{p}+b^{p})
for all
a,b\geq 0
and
1\leq p<\infty
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|){\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)|Z_{s}|}\biggr)^{p}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\beta)\left(t+\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|Z_{s}|\leq 1\}}{\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)|Z_{s}|}\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\beta)\left(t+\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|Z_{s}|\leq 1\}}{\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}|Z_{s}|^{2}}\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\right).

Here, the last inequality holds since
K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z|)^{3}|z|\leq C(\beta)
for all
|z|\leq 1
by the asymptotic expansion of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\searrow 0
. By the last inequality, (
4.10
) implies (
4.12
). The proof that (
4.9
) implies (
4.11
) is simpler, using again the asymptotic representations of
K_{0}(x)
and
K_{1}(x)
as
x\searrow 0
and as
x\nearrow\infty
.

The proof of Proposition
4.2
(2
∘
) (Section
4.2
) is also obtained by using
the explicit formula of the probability density of
Z_{t}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
, since we have to handle the singularity of the function
\mathscr{A}f(z^{1})
. The proof shares some similarities with that of Theorem
2.1
(1
∘
) on showing the continuity of convolutions of functions of weak integrability, but now the context allows a more general tool (Lemma
4.8
) to handle some steps of the proof.

For the proof of Proposition
4.2
(3
∘
) (Section
4.3
), the main task is to show

\displaystyle\forall\;z^{0}\in\mathbb{C},\;f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C}),\;0<t<\infty,\quad\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t+{\varepsilon}})]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})]}{{\varepsilon}}={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{t})].

(4.16)

The sufficiency of (
4.16
) for proving (
4.14
) is supported by two points:

•

By the definition (
4.13
) of
\mathscr{A}
and (
4.12
) with
p=1
, we get

s\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[|\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})|]\in L^{1}((0,t],{\mathrm{d}}s),\quad\forall\;0<t<\infty.

Moreover,
{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[|\mathscr{A}f(Z_{t})|]<\infty
for any
0<t<\infty
by (
4.11
).

•

Since
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{t})]
,
t>0
, is continuous [Proposition
4.2
(2
∘
)], (
4.16
) implies that the function
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})]
,
t>0
, is actually continuously differentiable with derivative
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{t})]
. This is due to the following elementary lemma, whose proof is omitted.

Lemma 4.4.

If
f:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is continuous such that its right-derivative
f^{\prime}_{+}
exists everywhere in
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
and is continuous, then
f(x)=f(0)+\int_{0}^{x}f^{\prime}_{+}(t){\mathrm{d}}t
for all
x\geq 0
.

Given (
4.16
) and the two points mentioned above, the required identity (
4.14
) follows:

{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})]=\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\left({\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{\varepsilon})]+\int_{\varepsilon}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})]{\mathrm{d}}s\right)=f(z^{0})+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})]{\mathrm{d}}s.

To obtain (
4.16
), we proceed with the following decomposition: for
0<s<t<\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{t-s}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s})]&=\frac{1}{t-s}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq t-s]\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{t-s}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}[f(Z_{t-s})-f(Z_{0});T_{0}(Z)>t-s];Z_{s}\neq 0],\end{split}

(4.17)

where
{\mathbb{E}}[Y;A]\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,{\mathbb{E}}[Y\mathds{1}_{A}]
, and

\vartheta_{s}:C_{\mathbb{C}}[0,\infty)\to C_{\mathbb{C}}[0,\infty)
denotes the shift operator such that
\vartheta_{s}(\omega){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\{\omega_{s+t};t\geq 0\}
. Note that (
4.17
) follows by using the Markov property of
\{Z_{t}\}
and the property that
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}(Z_{s}=0)=0
for all
0<s<\infty
due to (
2.5
). Given (
4.17
), we will show the following convergences:

(1)

The first term on the right-hand side of (
4.17
) converges uniformly to zero as
(t-s)\searrow 0
for
s_{0}\leq s<t\leq t_{0}
, for all fixed
0<s_{0}<t_{0}<\infty
(Proposition
4.9
).

(2)

The second term on the right-hand side of (
4.17
) converges to
{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})]
as
t\searrow s
for all fixed
s>0
(Proposition
4.15
).

These convergences will prove (
4.16
), and so, complete the proof of Proposition
4.2
(3
∘
). ∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2
(4
∘
).

By (
4.14
) and the Markov property of
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
,

\displaystyle M_{t}^{f}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{0})-\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s}){\mathrm{d}}s

(4.18)

is a continuous martingale if
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
, and hence, is a continuous local martingale by stopping if
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
. In particular, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{C}
,

M^{\theta}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,{\langle}Z_{t},\theta{\rangle}-{\langle}Z_{0},\theta{\rangle}+\int_{0}^{t}\biggl\langle\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)\overline{Z_{s}}},\theta\biggr\rangle{\mathrm{d}}s

is a continuous local martingale, and by taking
f(z)={\langle}z,\theta{\rangle}^{2}
in (
4.18
) and using a standard calculation with Itô’s formula (cf.
[
34
, the proof of (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) of (2.4) Proposition, p.297]
), we deduce that
{\langle}M^{\theta},M^{\theta}{\rangle}_{t}={\langle}\theta,\theta\rangle t
. Taking
\theta=1,{\mathtt{i}},1+{\mathtt{i}}
then shows that

W_{t}=W^{(1)}_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}W^{(2)}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,Z_{t}-Z_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|){\mathrm{d}}s}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)\overline{Z_{s}}},\quad\mbox{for $W^{(1)}_{t},W^{(2)}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}$},

is a continuous local martingale, and
{\langle}W^{(i)},W^{(j)}{\rangle}_{t}=\delta_{ij}t
, where
\delta_{ij}
is Kronecker’s delta. These properties are enough to prove
(
4.15
) since by Lévy’s characterization of Brownian motion
[
25
, 3.16 Theorem, p.157]
,
\{W_{t}\}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion.

\blacksquare

4.1
Sharp negative
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
-moments with logarithmic corrections

Our goal in Section
4.1
is to prove (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) of Proposition
4.2
(1
∘
). [We have explained in the above outline how (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) follow.]
We will first prove (
4.9
) along with finer properties in Proposition
4.5
and then finish with the proof of (
4.10
).
Recall
\log^{b}(a)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(\log a)^{b}
.

Proposition 4.5.

(1
∘
)
With a fixed constant
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}=\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta)
chosen from Lemma
4.6
, it holds that for all
0<t<\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{s\log^{2}s}+1\right)\in L^{1}([0,t],{\mathrm{d}}s),\end{split}

(4.19)

where
C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta,t)
increases in
t
. In particular, (
4.9
) holds.

(2
∘
)
The function
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
defined by (
1.8
) is continuous in
(0,\infty)
and satisfies, for any
0<t<1/2
,

\displaystyle C(\beta)(\tau\log^{2}\tau)^{-1}\leq{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\leq C_{\ref{eq:asympsbeta}}(\beta,t)(\tau\log^{2}\tau)^{-1},\quad\forall\;0<\tau\leq t,

(4.20)

where
C_{\ref{eq:asympsbeta}}(\beta,t)
increases in
t
.

Lemma 4.6.

There exists a constant
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}=\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta)\in(0,{\mathrm{e}}^{-3})
such that all of the following three functions are increasing on
(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}]
:
x\mapsto x|\log^{j}x|,\;j=2,3
, and
x\mapsto xK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}x)^{2}
.

Proof.
It suffices to note the following monotonicity properties:
x\mapsto x|\log^{2}x|
is increasing over
0<x\leq{\mathrm{e}}^{-2}
,
x\mapsto x|\log^{3}x|
is increasing over
0<x\leq{\mathrm{e}}^{-3}
, and
x\mapsto xK_{0}(x)^{2}
is increasing over all small enough
x>0
. The last monotonicity can be seen
by using the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.27
) of
K_{0}(x)
and
K_{1}(x)
as
x\to 0
since

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}x}xK_{0}(x)^{2}=K_{0}(x)^{2}+2xK_{0}(x)K_{0}^{\prime}(x)=K_{0}(x)[K_{0}(x)-2xK_{1}(x)].

(4.21)

See
[
29
, (5.7.9) on p.110]
for the derivative
K_{0}^{\prime}(x)=-K_{1}(x)
used in (
4.21
).

\blacksquare

In the following proof, we will use the standard fact that

\{|Z_{t}|^{2}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}
is a version of
{\rm BES}Q
of index
0
:
{\mathrm{d}}|Z_{t}|^{2}=2{\mathrm{d}}t+2|Z_{t}|{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{B}_{t}
for some one-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{\tilde{B}_{t}\}

[
34
, p.439]
, since
\{Z_{t}\}
is a two-dimensional Brownian motion under
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}
by definition. We denote the
{\rm BES}Q
of index
\nu
by
{\rm BES}Q(\nu)
.

Proof of Proposition
4.5
(1
∘
).

First, the
L^{1}
-property in (
4.19
) holds just because

\displaystyle\int\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{x\log^{2}x}=-\frac{1}{\log x}+C,\quad 0<x<1.

(4.22)

To obtain the inequality in (
4.19
), we consider
z^{0}=0
and
z^{0}\neq 0
separately in Steps 1 and 2 below. These steps will repeatedly use the following shorthand notation so that the expectation considered in (
4.19
) equals
{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[m(Z_{s})]
:

\displaystyle m(z^{1})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{4}},\quad z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}.

(4.23)

Also, note that
m(z^{1})\equiv m_{0}(|z^{1}|)
, where

\displaystyle m_{0}(x)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}x\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{x^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}x)^{4}}\quad\mbox{ is decreasing in $(0,\infty)$}.

(4.24)

This decreasing monotonicity holds since, for
0<x\leq y
,
\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}y\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}\leq\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}x\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}
and when
\sqrt{2\beta}y\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
,
xK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}x)^{2}\leq yK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}y)^{2}
by the choice of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
(Lemma
4.6
).

Step 1 (
\boldsymbol{z}^{\boldsymbol{0}}\boldsymbol{=}\boldsymbol{0}
).
We take three further steps below
to bound the right-hand side of the following inequality, which is obtained from (
2.5
) with
z^{0}=0
,
t=s
,
f\equiv m
and
g\equiv 1
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{s}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{s-\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau,

(4.25)

where
m(\cdot)
is defined by (
4.23
), and
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
is defined in (
1.8
). The following argument
views the right-hand side of (
4.25
) as a convolution of the two functions

\displaystyle\tau\mapsto{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\quad\&\quad\tau\mapsto\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}.

(4.26)

Step 1-1.
To bound the first function in (
4.26
), we rewrite the integral in (
1.8
) that defines
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)
, using the formula
\Gamma(u+1)=u\Gamma(u)
for
u>0
, and then consider two inequalities:

\displaystyle\forall\;0<\tau\leq t,\quad{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{u(\beta\tau)^{u}}{\Gamma(u+1)}{\mathrm{d}}u+4\pi\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\beta^{u}\tau^{u-1}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}u

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\pi\max\{\beta,1\}}{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u\tau^{u}{\mathrm{d}}u+4\pi\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\beta^{u}(t\vee 1)^{u-1}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}u

(4.28)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}+1\right),

(4.29)

where the last inequality follows because

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}ua^{u}{\mathrm{d}}u

\displaystyle=\frac{-a+a\log a+1}{\log^{2}a},\quad 0<a\neq 1,

(4.30)

\displaystyle C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,4\pi\max\left\{\beta,1,\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\beta^{u}(t\vee 1)^{u-1}}{\Gamma(u)}{\mathrm{d}}u\right\}.

(4.31)

Step 1-2.
The bound we wish to prove in Step 1-2 is for the second function in (
4.26
):

\displaystyle\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}\leq C(\beta)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}+1\right),\quad 0<\tau\leq t.

(4.32)

We first use the polar coordinates to get

\displaystyle\forall\;0<\tau<\infty,\quad\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}r}{\tau rK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)\int_{0}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}r}{\tau r|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|},

(4.33)

where the last inequality follows from the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
.
Next, we consider
\sqrt{\tau}\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
and
\sqrt{\tau}>\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
separately. For the case of
\sqrt{\tau}\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
, the integral on the right-hand side of (
4.33
) satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}r}{\tau r|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}r}{\tau r|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}+\int_{\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}r}{\tau r|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tau\log^{2}\sqrt{\tau}}+\frac{C(\beta)}{\sqrt{\tau}|\log^{3}(\sqrt{\tau})|}\int_{\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{1}{\tau}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}r.

(4.34)

Here, the first term in (
4.34
) follows by using
the identity

\displaystyle\int\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{-x\log^{3}(ax)}=\frac{1}{2\log^{2}(ax)}+C,\quad x>0,\;0<ax<1,

(4.35)

and the second term in (
4.34
) uses the increasing monotonicity of
x\mapsto x|\log^{3}x|
over
0<x\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
(Lemma
4.6
). By (
4.33
), (
4.34
) and the change of variables that replaces
r/\sqrt{\tau}
by
r
,

\displaystyle\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C(\beta)}{\tau\log^{2}\sqrt{\tau}}+\frac{C(\beta)}{\tau|\log^{3}(\sqrt{\tau})|}\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta\tau}}}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{r^{2}}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}r,

which is enough to prove (
4.32
) except that we only consider here
\sqrt{\tau}\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
. For the complementary case of
\sqrt{\tau}>\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
,
we obtain from (
4.33
) and (
4.35
) that

\int_{|z^{1}|\leq\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{P_{\tau}(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}}{|z^{1}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{3}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{\tau\log^{2}\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}\leq C(\beta)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}+1\right).

The proof of the whole statement of(
4.32
) is complete.

Step 1-3.

We now prove that for
C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-g2---1}}(\beta,t)
increasing in
t
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-g2---1}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{s\log^{2}s}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t.

(4.36)

First, it follows from (
4.25
), (
4.29
), and (
4.32
) that for all
0<s\leq t
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)C(\beta)\int_{0}^{s}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}+1\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s-\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)C(\beta)\left(2\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}{\mathrm{d}}\tau+s\right)\\
&\quad+C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)C(\beta)\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s-\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb2}}(\beta,t)C(\beta)\max\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}{\mathrm{d}}\tau+t,1\right\}\\
&\quad\times\left(1+\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s/2\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}\right),\end{split}

(4.37)

where
\int_{0}^{t}\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau/(\tau\log^{2}\tau)<\infty
by (
4.22
), and
(
4.37
) uses the choice of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
(Lemma
4.6
) to validate the following general bound: for any decreasing functions
f,g\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\forall\;0<s\leq t,\quad\int_{0}^{s}f(\tau)g(s-\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{s/2}f(\tau)g(s-\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\int_{s/2}^{s}f(\tau)g(s-\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq g(s/2)\int_{0}^{s/2}f(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+f(s/2)\int_{s/2}^{s}g(s-\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq g(s/2)\int_{0}^{t/2}f(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+f(s/2)\int_{0}^{t/2}g(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau.

(4.38)

Note that (
4.37
) is enough to prove (
4.36
).

Step 2 (
\boldsymbol{z}^{\boldsymbol{0}}\boldsymbol{\neq}\boldsymbol{0}
).
Our goal now is to extend
(
4.36
) to initial conditions
z^{0}\neq 0
such that the bounds obtained are uniform in
z^{0}\neq 0
. This way, we will prove the inequality in (
4.19
).

To obtain the required extension of (
4.36
),
recall that
m(\cdot)
is defined by (
4.23
), and let
m_{\beta}(\cdot)
be the transformation of
m(\cdot)
as defined below (
2.5
). Then we consider the following bound
implied by (
2.5
) and (
4.36
): for all
z^{0}\neq 0
and
0<s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\quad{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\quad\;+C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-g2---1}}(\beta,t)\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\\
&\quad\;+C_{\ref{case2}}(\beta,t)\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau.\end{split}

(4.39)

[We change “
C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-g2---1}}(\beta,t)
” and
“
(s-\tau)\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
” to “
C_{\ref{case2}}(\beta,t)
” and “
(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
,” respectively, to get the last inequality.] Below, Step 2-1 bounds the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.39
), Steps 2-2 bounds the last integral, and Step 2-3 gives the conclusion of Step 2.

Step 2-1.
We first note the following:

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(0)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\beta){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}\bigl|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)\bigr|}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(\beta){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}r\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{2s}){\mathrm{d}}r}{sr|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}.

(4.40)

Here, the first inequality holds by the comparison theorem of SDEs specialized to the case of
{\rm BES}Q(0)

[
25
, 2.18 Proposition, p.293]
since

K_{0}
is decreasing, and so,
for
m_{0}(\cdot)
defined by (
4.24
),
x\mapsto m_{0}(x)K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}x)
is also decreasing; the second inequality uses the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
and the notation that
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion; the equality uses the polar coordinates.

Next, we use (
4.40
) to show the following bound: for all
0<s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:0<|z^{0}|\leq 4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\leq C(\beta)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right).

(4.41)

[The reason for using
|z^{0}|\leq 4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}
in (
4.41
) will become clear in the next paragraph.] Under the assumption of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\geq(s/2)^{1/4}>0
, (
4.41
) can be seen by noting that the integral in (
4.40
) after a change of variables satisfies

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}r\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{2s}){\mathrm{d}}r}{sr|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}=\biggl(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta}2^{1/4}s^{1/4}}}+\int^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta s}}}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta}2^{1/4}s^{1/4}}}\biggr)\frac{\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{2}){\mathrm{d}}r}{sr|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta s}r)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s\log^{2}[(s/2)^{1/4}]}+\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{4}\biggl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta(s/2)^{1/2}}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr)\int^{\frac{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}{\sqrt{2\beta s}}}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta}2^{1/4}s^{1/4}}}\frac{\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{4}){\mathrm{d}}r}{sr|\log^{3}(\sqrt{2\beta s}r)|},

where the first term on the right-hand side uses (
4.35
), and
the second term can be bounded by
C(\beta)
. Also, for
s>0
such that
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}<(s/2)^{1/4}
, the right-hand side of (
4.40
) is bounded by
C(\beta)
, so (
4.41
) holds again. We have proved (
4.41
) for all
0<s\leq t
.

Next, we show that for all
0<s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:|z^{0}|>4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{Case2-5}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right),

(4.42)

where
C_{\ref{Case2-5}}(\beta,t)
increases in
t
.
The point of the proof now is to “subdue”
1/K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)
for large
|z^{0}|
, which is not needed for (
4.41
).
Specifically,
to see (
4.42
), note that
m_{0}(x)=m_{0}(x)\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}x\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}
by (
4.24
). Also, for
|z^{1}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}
and

|z^{0}|>4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}
,
|z^{0}-z^{1}|>|z^{0}|-|z^{1}|\geq 3|z^{0}|/4
. Hence, for
|z^{0}|>4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}
and
0<s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}\exp(-\frac{(3|z^{0}|/4)^{2}}{4s})P_{2s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}\exp(-\frac{(3|z^{0}|/4)^{2}}{4t})P_{2s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}.

(4.43)

Recall the asymptotic representation (
1.26
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to\infty
.
Since the proof of (
4.41
) effectively only bounds
P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})
, it extends to
P_{2s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})
and
we obtain from (
4.43
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:|z^{0}|>4\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}/\sqrt{2\beta}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{Case2-5}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{2s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{s\log^{2}s}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t,

which is enough to get the required inequality in (
4.42
) for all
0<s\leq t
.

In summary, since the last inequality and (
4.41
) are valid for all
0<s\leq t
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:z^{0}\neq 0}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{s}m_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\leq C_{\ref{Case2-6}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t.

(4.44)

In more detail,
C_{\ref{Case2-6}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
since
C_{\ref{Case2-5}}(\beta,t)
is.

Step 2-2.
Now we show that the integral term on the right-hand side of (
4.39
) satisfies

\displaystyle\quad\;\sup_{z^{0}:z^{0}\neq 0}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{Case2-2-3}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s/2)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t,

(4.45)

where
C_{\ref{Case2-2-3}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
. Below we consider the following four cases separately: (i)
s/2\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
and
s/2\leq|z^{0}|^{2}<\min\{1/(2\sqrt{2\beta}),1/4\}
; (ii)
s/2\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
,
s/2\leq|z^{0}|^{2}
and
|z^{0}|^{2}\geq\min\{1/(2\sqrt{2\beta}),1/4\}
; (iii)
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\geq s/2>|z^{0}|^{2}
; (iv)
s/2>\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
.

For the case of
s/2\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
and
s/2\leq|z^{0}|^{2}<\min\{1/(2\sqrt{2\beta}),1/4\}
, we first note that

\displaystyle{\rm argmax}\{\tau^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-a/\tau};\tau>0\}=a,\quad\forall\;a>0,

(4.46)

since
({\mathrm{d}}/{\mathrm{d}}x)x{\mathrm{e}}^{-ax}={\mathrm{e}}^{-ax}(1-ax)
. Hence,
\tau\mapsto P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})
over
0<\tau<\infty
can be bounded by
P_{2\cdot|z^{0}|^{2}/2}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})
. By this bound, Lemma
2.2
and the formula in (
4.22
), we get

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\times\frac{1}{4\pi|z^{0}|^{2}/2}\exp\left(-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{2\cdot|z^{0}|^{2}/2}\right)\frac{1}{|\log s|}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|z^{0}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\times\frac{1}{|\log|z^{0}|^{2}|}+1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{|z^{0}|^{2}\log^{2}(|z^{0}|^{2})}+1\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1,

(4.47)

where the second inequality uses the bound
\sup_{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}|\frac{\log(s/2)}{\log s}|\leq C(\beta)
and the decreasing monotonicity of
x\mapsto|\log x|
over
0<x\leq 1
,
the third inequality uses the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
,
and the last inequality can be seen by considering separately
|z^{0}|^{2}\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
and
|z^{0}|^{2}>\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
and using the choice of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
(Lemma
4.6
).

For the case of
s/2\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
,
s/2\leq|z^{0}|^{2}
and
|z^{0}|^{2}\geq\min\{1/(2\sqrt{2\beta}),1/4\}
, we consider

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{4t}}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{4\tau}}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{(4\pi\tau){}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C_{\ref{Case2-2-1-1}}(\beta,t)

(4.48)

for all
0<s\leq t
, where
C_{\ref{Case2-2-1-1}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
.

For the case of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\geq s/2>|z^{0}|^{2}
, we use a slight modification of the proof of (
4.38
), the decreasing monotonicity of
\tau\mapsto P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})
over
\tau>|z^{0}|^{2}/2
by (
4.46
), and the decreasing monotonicity of
K_{0}
to get the first inequality below:

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s/2)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right)\int_{0}^{s/2}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\;+\frac{P_{s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta(s/2)})}}\int_{s/2}^{s}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{Case2-2-2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s/2)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s/2)\log^{2}(s/2)}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t,

(4.49)

where
C_{\ref{Case2-2-2}}(\beta,t)
increases in
t
.
Note that (
4.49
) holds since each term on its left-hand side can be bounded by its right-hand side
except with a different constant that increases in
t
. In more detail,
we apply Lemma
2.2
to bound the first integral on the left-hand side of (
4.49
) and use
(
4.22
),
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\geq s/2
, and the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
to get

\frac{P_{s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta(s/2)})}}\int_{s/2}^{s}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{s\log[\sqrt{2\beta(s/2)}\wedge\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}]\log(s/2)},

and the increasing monotonicity of
1/K_{0}(\cdot)
gives

\frac{P_{s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta(s/2)})}}\int_{s/2}^{s}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\frac{1}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta(t/2)})}}.

Finally, for the case of
s/2>\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
, by writing
\int_{0}^{s}=\int_{0}^{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}+\int_{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}^{s}
, we get

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{s}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}}{{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)\int_{0}^{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}\frac{P_{2\tau}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0}){\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta\tau}2\pi}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\;+\frac{C(\beta){\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{2t}}}{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}}^{s}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{(s-\tau)\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(s-\tau)\log^{2}(s-\tau)}+1\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{Case2-2-3-1}}(\beta,t),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t,

(4.50)

where the last inequality uses Lemma
2.2
and (
4.22
).
We have obtained (
4.45
) by establishing (
4.47
), (
4.48
), (
4.49
) and (
4.50
) for the four cases mentioned below (
4.45
).

Step 2-3.
Applying (
4.44
) and (
4.45
) to (
4.39
) proves

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:z^{0}\neq 0}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{|\sqrt{2\beta}Z_{s}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{s}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)^{4}}\right]\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-g2---2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{s\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{s\log^{2}s}+1\right),\quad\forall\;0<s\leq t.

(4.51)

By (
4.36
) and (
4.51
), we have proved the inequality in
(
4.19
) for all
0<s\leq t
. The proof of Proposition
4.5
(1
∘
) is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
4.5
(2
∘
).

For
0<\tau\leq 1/2
, (
4.27
) shows that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4\pi}{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{u(\beta\tau)^{u}}{\Gamma(u+1)}{\mathrm{d}}u\gtrsim\frac{\min\{\beta,1\}}{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u\tau^{u}{\mathrm{d}}u

\displaystyle=\frac{\min\{\beta,1\}}{\tau}\biggl(\frac{-\tau+\tau\log\tau+1}{\log^{2}\tau}\biggr)\geq\frac{C(\beta)}{\tau\log^{2}\tau},

(4.52)

where the equality uses (
4.30
), and the last inequality uses the fact that
\tau\mapsto-\tau+\tau\log\tau+1
is decreasing in
(0,1)
.
By the last inequality and (
4.29
), we obtain the required two-sided bound in (
4.20
) for
0<t<1/2
. Also, the continuity of
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
in
(0,\infty)
follows immediately from the definition (
1.8
) of
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}
by using the dominated convergence theorem.

\blacksquare

The following proof completes the proof of Proposition
4.2
(1
∘
).

Proof of (
4.10
) of Proposition
4.2
.

It is enough to
prove

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\exp\left\{\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{r}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|)^{4}}{\mathrm{d}}r\right\}\right]<\infty,\quad\forall\;t,\lambda\in(0,\infty).

We first consider the following approximations with
{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{s}g_{\varepsilon}(|Z_{r}|){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\right],\quad\mbox{where }g_{\varepsilon}(y)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\lambda\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}(y\vee{\varepsilon})\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(y\vee{\varepsilon})^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}(y\vee{\varepsilon}))^{4}},

such that
f_{\varepsilon}
satisfies the following inequality:

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(s)\leq 1+\int_{0}^{s}\lambda C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{r\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{r\log^{2}r}+1\right)f_{\varepsilon}(s-r){\mathrm{d}}r,\quad\forall\;0\leq s\leq t.

(4.53)

To see (
4.53
), note that since
m_{0}(\cdot)
in (
4.24
) is decreasing,

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}(y)\leq\lambda\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}y\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{y^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}y)^{4}},\quad\forall\;y\geq 0.

(4.54)

Then by the expansion
{\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{0}^{s}h(r){\mathrm{d}}r}=1+\int_{0}^{s}h(r){\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{r}^{s}h(v){\mathrm{d}}v}{\mathrm{d}}r
and the Markov property of
\{Z_{t}\}
,

\displaystyle\quad{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{s}g_{\varepsilon}(|Z_{r}|){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{s}\lambda\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{|Z_{r}|^{2}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|)^{4}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{r}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{s-r}g_{\varepsilon}(|Z_{v}|){\mathrm{d}}v\right)\right]{\mathrm{d}}r\right],

(4.55)

which leads to (
4.53
) upon applying (
4.19
).

Now, (
4.53
) shows a convolution-type inequality such that

r\mapsto\lambda C_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{r\leq 2\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{r\log^{2}r}+1\right)

is independent of
{\varepsilon}
and is in
L^{1}([0,t],{\mathrm{d}}r)
by (
4.19
). Also,
\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}f_{\varepsilon}(s)<\infty
since
g_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})
. Hence, an extension of Grönwall’s lemma (e.g.
[
15
, Lemma 15 on pp.22–23]
) gives

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}f_{\varepsilon}(s)\leq C(\beta,\lambda,t),\quad\forall\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,1).

(4.56)

Moreover, by Fatou’s lemma, passing
{\varepsilon}\searrow 0
for the left-hand side of (
4.56
) leads to the required bound (
4.10
) for
\lambda>0
. In more detail, we have used the fact that
\{|Z_{t}|\}\sim{\rm BES}(0,\beta{\downarrow})
is instantaneously reflecting at
0

[
18
, Theorem 2.1, p.883]
, so
\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\{Z_{r}=0\}}{\mathrm{d}}r=0
.

\blacksquare

4.2
Continuity of the forward derivative

To obtain Proposition
4.2
(2
∘
), it suffices to prove the following continuity property.

Proposition 4.7.

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}(\mathbb{C})
and
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, the following function is continuous:

\displaystyle t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)\overline{Z_{t}}}f(Z_{t})\biggr],\quad t>0.

(4.57)

The proof of Proposition
4.7
considers the expectations in (
4.57
) via the analytical formulas in (
2.2
). To handle the convolution integrals of functions of weak integrability in these formulas, we now use the following lemma, which seems difficult to find in the literature.

Lemma 4.8.

Fix
0<T<\infty
. Let
f,g:(0,T)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be such that
f
is bounded on compacts in
(0,T)
,
g
is continuous in
(0,T)
, and
f,g\in L^{1}((0,T))
. Then
f\star g(t)=\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s
is in
L^{1}((0,T))
and is continuous in
(0,T)
.

Proof.
By writing
\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{0}^{t/2}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{t/2}^{t}f(s)g(t-s)
, we obtain immediately from the assumptions imposed on
f,g
that
\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s
defines an absolutely convergent integral for all
0<t<T
. The proof that
t\mapsto\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s\in L^{1}((0,T))
is standard.
Hence, it remains to prove the continuity of
f\star g
in
(0,T)
. In the following,
we fix
t\in(0,T)
and write
S(t_{1},t_{2}){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\sup_{t_{1}\leq r\leq t_{2}}f(r)
. Note that
S(t_{1},t_{2})<\infty
for
0<t_{1}\leq t_{2}<T
.

We first show the right-continuity of
f\star g
at
t
. Let
0<\delta<t
with
t+\delta<T
, and write

\displaystyle f\star g(t+\delta)-f\star g(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t+\delta}f(s)g(t+\delta-s){\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{t+\delta}f(s)g(t+\delta-s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}f(s)[g(t+\delta-s)-g(t-s)]{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.58)

The last two integrals can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+\delta}f(s)g(t+\delta-s){\mathrm{d}}s\leq S(t,t+\delta)\int_{0}^{\delta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s,

(4.59)

and for any
0<\eta<\min\{t/4,(T-t)/4\}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|\int_{0}^{t}f(s)[g(t+\delta-s)-g(t-s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{\eta}f(t-s)[g(\delta+s)-g(s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|+\left|\int_{\eta}^{t}f(t-s)[g(\delta+s)-g(s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq S(t-\eta,t)\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s\right)+\int_{0}^{t}f(s){\mathrm{d}}s\sup_{\eta\leq s\leq t}|g(\delta+s)-g(s)|.\end{split}

(4.60)

By (
4.58
)–(
4.60
), we get, for all
0<\delta<t
with
t+\delta<T
and
0<\eta<\min\{t/4,(T-t)/4\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|f\star g(t+\delta)-f\star g(t)|&\leq S(t,t+\delta)\int_{0}^{\delta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s\\
