&\quad+S(t-\eta,t)\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s\right)\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}f(s){\mathrm{d}}s\sup_{\eta\leq s\leq t}|g(\delta+s)-g(s)|.\end{split}

(4.61)

Now, given
{\varepsilon}>0
, the continuity of
\tau\mapsto\int_{0}^{\tau}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s
implies the existence of
0<\eta_{0}<\min\{t/4,(T-t)/4\}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\eta_{0}}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s<\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4[S(t/4,t+3(T-t)/4)+1]},\quad\forall\;0\leq\tau\leq t.

(4.62)

Also, since
g
is continuous in
(0,T)
, we can find
0<\delta_{0}<\eta_{0}
with
t+\delta_{0}<T
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta_{0}\leq s\leq t}|g(\delta+s)-g(s)|<\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4[\int_{0}^{t}f(s){\mathrm{d}}s+1]},\quad\forall\;0<\delta<\delta_{0}.

(4.63)

By applying (
4.62
) and (
4.63
) to (
4.61
) with
\eta=\eta_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle|f\star g(t+\delta)-f\star g(t)|\leq\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}\cdot 2+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}={\varepsilon},\quad\forall\;0<\delta<\delta_{0}.

The foregoing inequality proves the required right-continuity of
f\star g
in
(0,T)
.

The left-continuity of
f\star g
in
(0,T)
can be obtained similarly. For
0<\delta<t/2
, write

\displaystyle f\star g(t)-f\star g(t-\delta)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t-\delta}f(s)g(t-\delta-s){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\delta}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t-\delta}f(s)[g(t-s)-g(t-\delta-s)]{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.64)

The last two integrals can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{t-\delta}^{t}f(s)g(t-s){\mathrm{d}}s\leq S(t-\delta,t)\int_{0}^{\delta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s,

(4.65)

and for any
0<\eta<\min\{t/4,(T-t)/4\}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|\int_{0}^{t-\delta}f(s)[g(t-s)-g(t-\delta-s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{\eta}f(t-\delta-s)[g(\delta+s)-g(s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|+\left|\int_{\eta}^{t-\delta}f(t-\delta-s)[g(\delta+s)-g(s)]{\mathrm{d}}s\right|

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq S(t-\delta-\eta,t-\delta)\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{\eta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s\right)\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}f(s){\mathrm{d}}s\sup_{\eta\leq s\leq t}|g(\delta+s)-g(s)|.\end{split}

(4.66)

Then for the same choice of
\eta_{0}
and
\delta_{0}
from (
4.62
) and (
4.63
), we obtain from (
4.64
), (
4.65
) and (
4.66
) that, for all
0<\delta<\delta_{0}
,

\displaystyle|f\star g(t)-f\star g(t-\delta)|

\displaystyle\leq S(t-\delta,t)\int_{0}^{\delta}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s+S(t-\delta-\eta_{0},t-\delta)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+\eta_{0}}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{\eta_{0}}g(s){\mathrm{d}}s\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}f(s){\mathrm{d}}s\sup_{\eta_{0}\leq s\leq t}|g(\delta+s)-g(s)|

\displaystyle\leq{\varepsilon},

which is the required left-continuity of
f\star g
at
t
. The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
4.7
.

By (
1.8
) and (
2.5
), the expectations in (
4.57
) satisfy

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)\overline{Z_{t}}}f(Z_{t})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{\mathbb{C}}P_{t}(z^{0},z^{1})\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{\overline{z}^{1}}f(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}+\\
\vskip-5.69046pt\\
\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{0}^{t}P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})\int_{0}^{t-s}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\\
\vskip-5.69046pt\\
\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\int_{\mathbb{C}}P_{t-s-\tau}(z^{1})\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{\overline{z}^{1}}f(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}s,&z^{0}\neq 0,\\
\vskip-5.69046pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{C}}P_{t-\tau}(z^{1})\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{\overline{z}^{1}}f(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau,&z^{0}=0.\end{cases}

Note that
s\mapsto P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})
,
s>0
, is bounded continuous whenenver
z^{0}\neq 0
. Hence, by Proposition
4.5
(2
∘
) and Lemma
4.8
, the required continuity of the function in (
4.57
) holds as soon as we prove the following two properties:

\displaystyle t\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{C}}P_{t}(z^{0},z^{1})\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{\overline{z}^{1}}f(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1},\;t>0,\;\mbox{ is continuous},\;\forall\;z^{0}\in\mathbb{C},\;

(4.67)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}P_{t}(z^{1})\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}{\overline{z}^{1}}f(z^{1}){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}\leq\frac{C(\beta,f)}{t^{1/2}},\quad t>0.

(4.68)

We show (
4.67
) and (
4.68
) now.
To get (
4.67
), it suffices to note that by using the polar coordinates and the asymptotic representations (
1.27
) and (
1.28
) of
K_{1}(x)
as
x\to 0
and as
x\to\infty
,

z^{1}\mapsto[\widehat{K}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)/\overline{z}^{1}]f(z^{1})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{C})
, and so, the required continuity follows by dominated convergence. Also, (
4.68
) holds by using the following three properties:
K_{1}(x)\lesssim x^{-1}
for all
x>0
[recall (
1.27
)–(
1.28
)], the Brownian scaling, and
{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}[|Z_{1}|^{-1}]<\infty
. The proof of Proposition
4.7
is complete.

\blacksquare

4.3
Kolmogorov’s forward equation under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
4.2
(3
∘
), by which we will complete the proof of Proposition
4.2
. Recall that by (
4.16
), it is enough to prove the convergences of the two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.17
) in the particular modes of convergence described below (
4.17
). The convergences will be obtained in Sections
4.3.1
and
4.3.2
as Propositions
4.9
and
4.15
.

4.3.1
Differentiation across the zeros

Proposition 4.9.

For all
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{C})
and
0<s_{0}<t_{0}<\infty
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\sup_{s_{0}\leq s<t=s+{\varepsilon}\leq t_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,\;T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}]\right|=0.

(4.69)

The proof of this proposition needs Lemma
4.10
–
4.14
stated below.
For the first lemma, we use several ingredients from Section
4.1
. In particular, recall that
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}=\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}(\beta)
is an auxiliary constant from Lemma
4.6
.

Lemma 4.10.

For all
t>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon})\leq C_{\ref{bdd:PiZbdd2}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{1}{t}+1\right){\varepsilon}^{2}|\log^{3}{\varepsilon}|,

(4.70)

where
C_{\ref{bdd:PiZbdd2}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
.

Remark 4.11.

(
4.70
) cannot be improved to one where the bound is
C_{\ref{bdd:PiZbdd2}}(\beta,t){\varepsilon}^{2}|\log^{3}{\varepsilon}|
. This necessity can be seen by taking
z^{0}=0
.∎

Proof of Lemma
4.10
.

We consider
z^{0}=0
and
z^{0}\neq 0
in Steps 1 and 2, respectively.

Step 1.
For the case of
z^{0}=0
, take
g\equiv 1
and
f(z^{1})\equiv\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}\}}
in (
2.5
). Then for all
t>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
,

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\int_{\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}}P_{t-\tau}(z^{1})K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|){\mathrm{d}}z^{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{ineq:BESb0-2}}(\beta,t)\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{\tau\log^{2}\tau}+1\right){\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}\tau,

(4.71)

where (
4.71
) uses Proposition
4.5
(2
∘
) and the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
,
\{Z_{t}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion, and
C_{\ref{ineq:BESb0-2}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
. Note that we can use (
4.38
) to bound the right-hand side of (
4.71
). Specifically, since
x\mapsto|\log x|
is decreasing on
0<x\leq 1
, and
\tau\mapsto\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta\tau}|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}\}}
is decreasing for any fixed
z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}
, the following function is decreasing:

\tau\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta\tau}|Z_{1}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta\tau}|Z_{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr].

Also,
\tau\mapsto\mathds{1}_{\{\tau\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}/(\tau\log^{2}\tau)
is decreasing by the choice of
\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}
(Lemma
4.6
).
Hence, by (
4.38
) with
s=t
and then (
4.22
), (
4.71
) implies, for
C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd1}}(\beta,t)
increasing in
t
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\;{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon})\\
&\leq C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd1}}(\beta,t)\biggl(\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\frac{t}{2}\leq\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}\}}}{(\frac{t}{2})\log^{2}(\frac{t}{2})}+1\biggr)\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
&\quad+C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd1}}(\beta,t){\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t/2}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t/2}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr],\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}).\end{aligned}

(4.72)

Let us bound the two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.72
). For the first term, write

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]\\
&=\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}{\tau}r\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}\right){\mathrm{d}}r,\quad\tau>0,\end{split}

(4.73)

by the polar coordinates. Hence, for all
t>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\frac{|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|}{\tau}r\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\tau}\right){\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|\cdot r\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{r^{2}}}\frac{1}{\tau}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2\tau}\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|\cdot r\left[\log^{+}\left(\frac{\max\{t,1\}}{r^{2}}\right)+1\right]{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)(\log^{+}t+1)\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta}}}\log^{2}(\sqrt{2\beta}r)r{\mathrm{d}}r

(4.74)

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)(\log^{+}t+1){\varepsilon}^{2}\log^{2}{\varepsilon}.

(4.75)

Here, the equality in the second line changes the order of integration and then changes variables by replacing
\tau/r^{2}
with
\tau
;
(
4.74
) holds since
1\leq C(\beta)|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}r)|
for
0<r\leq{\varepsilon}/\sqrt{2\beta}
due to the assumption
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
; (
4.75
) uses

\int x\log^{2}x{\mathrm{d}}x=4^{-1}x^{2}(2\log^{2}x-2\log x+1)+C
,
x>0
.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
4.72
), note that
for all
\tau>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
,

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta\tau}}}|\log(\sqrt{2\beta\tau}r)|r\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}}{2}\right){\mathrm{d}}r

(4.76)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{2\beta\tau}}}|\log(\sqrt{2\beta\tau}r)|r{\mathrm{d}}r=\frac{1}{2\beta\tau}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(-\log r)r{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\beta\tau}\cdot\frac{1}{4}{\varepsilon}^{2}(1-2\log{\varepsilon}),

(4.77)

where the last equality uses the identity

\int x\log x{\mathrm{d}}x=\frac{1}{4}x^{2}(2\log x-1)+C
,
x>0
.
Then by (
4.77
) with
\tau=t/2
, the following holds for all
t>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t/2}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t/2}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{t}({\varepsilon}^{2}-{\varepsilon}^{2}\log{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{t}{\varepsilon}^{2}|\log{\varepsilon}|.

(4.78)

In summary, applying (
4.75
) and (
4.78
) to (
4.72
) proves the following inequality:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon})\leq C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{1}{t}+1\right){\varepsilon}^{2}\log^{2}{\varepsilon},\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}),

(4.79)

where
C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final}}(\beta,t)
is increasing in
t
.

Step 2.

For the case of
z^{0}\neq 0
, we choose the following
(g,f)
for (
2.5
):
g\equiv 1
and
f(z^{1})\equiv\tilde{f}(z^{1})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}\}}
. The first term from this use of (
2.5
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}P_{t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{0}}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(|\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr].

(4.80)

The last equality holds
by using the comparison theorem of SDEs
[
25
, 2.18 Proposition, p.293]
specialized to the case of
{\rm BES}Q(0)
,
since
|z|\mapsto|\log|z||\mathds{1}_{\{|z|\leq{\varepsilon}\}}
is decreasing.

We show that (
4.80
) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\neq 0}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}P_{t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\leq\frac{C_{\ref{Zibdd:part1}}(\beta,t)}{t}{\varepsilon}^{2}|\log{\varepsilon}|,\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})

(4.81)

for some
C_{\ref{Zibdd:part1}}(\beta,t)
increasing in
t
.
First, by (
4.78
) and the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
, (
4.80
) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:|z^{0}|\leq 4{\varepsilon}/\sqrt{2\beta}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}P_{t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{t}{\varepsilon}^{2}|\log{\varepsilon}|,\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}).

(4.82)

Note that for
|z^{0}|>4{\varepsilon}/\sqrt{2\beta}
and
|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}/\sqrt{2\beta}
, we have
|z^{0}-z^{1}|\geq|z^{0}|-|z^{1}|\geq 3|z^{0}|/4
. Hence, the definition of
\tilde{f}
above (
4.80
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}P_{t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}\exp(-\frac{(3|z^{0}|/4)^{2}}{4t})P_{2t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}\exp(-\frac{(3|z^{0}|/4)^{2}}{4t})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{0}}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{2t}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{2t}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}\exp(-\frac{(3|z^{0}|/4)^{2}}{4t})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{2t}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{2t}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr],

(4.83)

where the second line follows from the asymptotic representation (
1.25
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
, and the last inequality uses the comparison theorem of SDEs for
{\rm BES}Q(0)
. Note that by the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}\neq 0}\frac{\exp(-\theta\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{t})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}<\infty,\quad\forall\;t>0,\;\theta\in(0,\infty).

(4.84)

By (
4.78
), (
4.83
) and (
4.84
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{z^{0}:|z^{0}|>4{\varepsilon}/\sqrt{2\beta}}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta t}P_{t}\tilde{f}_{\beta}(z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\leq\frac{C_{\ref{PiZ:bddEE0000}}(\beta,t)}{t}{\varepsilon}^{2}|\log{\varepsilon}|,\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})

(4.85)

for some
C_{\ref{PiZ:bddEE0000}}(\beta,t)
increasing in
t
. Combining (
4.82
) and (
4.85
) proves (
4.81
).

To deal with the second term from (
2.5
) for
g\equiv 1
,
f(z^{1})\equiv\mathds{1}_{\{\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|\leq{\varepsilon}\}}
and
z^{0}\neq 0
, we note that (
4.76
) shows

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}\bigl[\bigl|\log(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|)\bigr|;\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon}\bigr]\leq C(\beta)(|\log\tau|+1),\quad\forall\;\tau>0,

(4.86)

so that by replacing (
4.78
) with (
4.86
) in the proof of (
4.79
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\tau}|\leq{\varepsilon})\leq C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-prel-1000}}(\beta,\tau)(|\log\tau|+1),\quad\forall\;\tau>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}),

(4.87)

for
C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-prel-1000}}(\beta,\tau)
increasing in
\tau
.
Then that second term from (
2.5
) just mentioned satisfies the following bounds, where the first inequality below uses (
4.79
) and (
4.87
):

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{0}^{t}\frac{2\pi{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-s}|\leq{\varepsilon}){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{0}^{t/2}+\int_{t/2}^{(t-{\varepsilon}^{2})\vee(t/2)}+\int_{(t-{\varepsilon}^{2})\vee(t/2)}^{t}\biggr)\frac{2\pi{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}

\displaystyle\quad\times{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta{\downarrow}}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{t-s}|\leq{\varepsilon}){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-1000}}(\beta,t)\int_{0}^{t/2}\frac{2\pi{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta s}P_{2s}({\sqrt{2}}z^{0})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{t-s}+1\right){\varepsilon}^{2}\log^{2}{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\quad\;+\frac{C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-1000}}(\beta,t)\exp(-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{2t})}{tK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{t/2}^{(t-{\varepsilon}^{2})\vee(t/2)}\left(\frac{1}{t-s}+1\right){\varepsilon}^{2}\log^{2}{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\quad\;+\frac{C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-1000}}(\beta,t)\exp(-\frac{|z^{0}|^{2}}{2t})}{tK_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{0}|)}\int_{(t-{\varepsilon}^{2})\vee(t/2)}^{t}(|\log(t-s)|+1){\mathrm{d}}s\end{split}

(4.88)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1}}(\beta,t)\left(\frac{1}{t}+1\right){\varepsilon}^{2}|\log^{3}{\varepsilon}|,\quad\forall\;t>0,\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}}),\;z^{0}\neq 0,

(4.89)

where
C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1-1000}}(\beta,t)
and
C_{\ref{PiZ:bdd3-final1}}(\beta,t)
are increasing in
t
. In more detail, to get (
4.89
), we have used Lemma
2.2
to bound the first integral on the left-hand side of (
4.89
). Also, to bound the last two terms on
the left-hand side of (
4.89
), we have used (
4.84
) to bound the coefficients of the two integrals,
and the identity
\int\log x{\mathrm{d}}x=x(\log x-1)+C
for
x>0
has been applied to bound the last integral on the left-hand side of (
4.89
).

Recall that the leftmost sides of (
4.83
) and (
4.89
) arise from using
g\equiv 1
and
f(z^{1})\equiv\tilde{f}(z^{1})
for (
2.5
) in the case of
z^{0}\neq 0
. Hence,
combining (
4.79
), (
4.81
), and (
4.89
) proves (
4.70
) for all
t>0
and
{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
. The proof of Lemma
4.10
is complete.

\blacksquare

For the next two lemmas, let
{\rm BES}Q(0,\beta{\downarrow})
denote
\{|Z_{t}|^{2}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
or other processes with the same distribution. The SDE of
{\rm BES}Q(0,\beta{\downarrow})
is

\displaystyle X_{t}=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}2\biggl(1-\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}\sqrt{|X_{s}|})}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}\sqrt{|X_{s}|})}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s+2\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{|X_{s}|}{\mathrm{d}}B_{s}

(4.90)

for a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{B_{t}\}

[
11
, Theorem 2.15 (1
∘
)]
.

Lemma 4.12.

For any solution to (
4.90
) with
X_{0}\geq 0
, there exists a version of
{\rm BES}Q(0)

\{X^{\prime}_{t}\}
such that with probability one,
X_{t}\leq X^{\prime}_{t}
for all
t
.

Proof.
First, we use the strong well-posedness of the SDE of
{\rm BES}Q(0)
to construct a nonnegative process
\{X^{\prime}_{t}\}
such that
X^{\prime}_{0}=X_{0}
and
{\mathrm{d}}X^{\prime}_{t}=2{\mathrm{d}}t+2\sqrt{|X_{t}^{\prime}|}{\mathrm{d}}B_{t}
with respect to the same
\{B_{t}\}
from (
4.90
). (See
[
23
, Theorem 3.2 of Chapter IV, p.182]
for
a general theorem that guarantees the strong well-posedness of the SDE of
{\rm BES}Q(0)
.)
Second, since the drift coefficient of (
4.90
) is pointwise bounded by the constant
2
, the required property follows from a general comparison theorem of Ikeda and Watanabe (cf.
[
23
, Theorem 1.1 of Chapter VI, pp.437–438]
).

\blacksquare

The next lemma concerns the Hölder continuity of
\{|Z_{t}|^{2}\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}
.

Lemma 4.13.

It holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\biggl[\biggl(\sup_{0\leq s\neq t\leq T}\frac{||Z_{t}|^{2}-|Z_{s}|^{2}|}{|t-s|^{\eta}}\biggr)^{2\nu}\biggr]<\infty,\\
&\forall\;\nu>1,\;\eta\in[0,\tfrac{\nu-1}{2\nu}),\;T\in(0,\infty),\;z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}.\end{split}

(4.91)

Proof.
It is enough to show the following bound: for all
\nu>1
,
T\in(0,\infty)
and
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[||Z_{t}|^{2}-|Z_{s}|^{2}|^{2\nu}]\leq C(\beta,\nu,|z^{0}|,T)(t-s)^{\nu},\quad\forall\;0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

(4.92)

Specifically, given this bound, (
4.91
) follows upon applying the Kolmogorov continuity theorem
[
34
, (2.1) Theorem, p.26]
.

To validate (
4.92
), we use the SDE (
4.90
) for
X_{t}=|Z_{t}|^{2}
, which reads

\displaystyle|Z_{t}|^{2}=|Z_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}2\biggl(1-\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s+2\int_{0}^{t}|Z_{s}|{\mathrm{d}}B_{s}

(4.93)

By the asymptotic representations (
1.25
)–(
1.28
) of
K_{0}
and
K_{1}

as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\widehat{K}}_{1}(x)}{K_{0}(x)}\lesssim 1+x,\quad x>0.

(4.94)

Hence, for all
\nu>1
,
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[||Z_{t}|^{2}-|Z_{s}|^{2}|^{2\nu}]

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu)(t-s)^{2\nu}+C(\beta,\nu){\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{r}|{\mathrm{d}}r\biggr)^{2\nu}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+C(\nu){\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{r}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}r\biggr)^{\nu}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu)(t-s)^{2\nu}+C(\beta,\nu)(t-s)^{2\nu-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{r}|^{2\nu}{\mathrm{d}}r\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C(\nu)(t-s)^{\nu-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{r}|^{2\nu}{\mathrm{d}}r\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,\nu,|z^{0}|,T)(t-s)^{\nu},

(4.95)

which is the required bound in (
4.92
).
In more detail, the first inequality above uses the inequality
(x+y)^{2\nu}\leq C(\nu)(x^{2\nu}+y^{2\nu})
for all
x,y\geq 0
and
the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
34
, (4.1) Theorem, p.160]
. Also, the second inequality in the above display follows by using Hölder’s inequality with the pairs of Hölder conjugates
(p,q)=(2\nu,\frac{2\nu}{2\nu-1})
and
(p,q)=(\nu,\frac{\nu}{\nu-1})
. Finally, (
4.95
) follows since
{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[|Z_{r}|^{2\nu}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{0}}[|Z_{r}|^{2\nu}]
by Lemma
4.12
and

{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{0}}[|Z_{r}|^{2\nu}]
is bounded in
0\leq r\leq T
. The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

The last lemma prepares the forthcoming application of (
4.91
).

Lemma 4.14.

For every
\nu\in(1,\infty)
satisfying

\displaystyle\frac{3\nu+1}{4\nu-1}<1,

(4.96)

there exist
\eta\in\left(0,\frac{\nu-1}{2\nu}\right)
and
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}<2\gamma.

(4.97)

Proof.
Note that (
4.96
) holds if and only if
\nu>2
. To justify the existence of
(\eta,\gamma)
, first note

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\eta_{0}}{2}\right)\Big|_{\eta_{0}=\frac{\nu-1}{2\nu}}\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{3\nu+1}{4\nu}\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{3\nu+1}{4\nu-1}<\frac{1}{2},

(4.98)

where the last inequality uses (
4.96
). By (
4.98
), we can choose
\eta\in\left(0,\frac{\nu-1}{2\nu}\right)
such that

\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}<\frac{1}{2}
. Hence, we can choose
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that

\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}<\gamma
, which is enough to get (
4.97
).

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
4.9
.

For
0<s_{0}\leq s<t\leq t_{0}
with
{\varepsilon}=t-s<1
, write

\displaystyle\quad\;\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\;+\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[f(Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}\right].

Hence, by the Lipschitz continuity of
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}]\right|

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq\frac{C(f)}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[|Z_{t}-Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}|;Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}]\\
&\quad\;+\frac{C(f)}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[|Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}-Z_{s}|;Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}].\end{split}

(4.99)

We now turn to Lemmas
4.13
and
4.14
to handle (
4.99
).
When
Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}=0
, we have

|Z_{t}-Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}|=|Z_{t}|=||Z_{t}|^{2}|^{1/2}=||Z_{t}|^{2}-|Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}|^{2}|^{1/2},

and similarly,

|Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}-Z_{s}|=||Z_{s+T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}}|^{2}-|Z_{s}|^{2}|^{1/2}
. Hence, for
\nu\in(1,\infty)
satisfying (
4.96
) and for
(\eta,\gamma)
chosen in Lemma
4.14
, (
4.99
) implies

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[f(Z_{t})-f(Z_{s});Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(f)}{{\varepsilon}^{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\Biggl[\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq t_{0}}}{{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}}}}\frac{||Z_{s_{2}}|^{2}-|Z_{s_{1}}|^{2}|^{1/2}}{{\varepsilon}^{\frac{\eta}{2}}};Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(f)}{{\varepsilon}^{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\Biggl[\Biggl(\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq t_{0}}}{{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}}}}\frac{||Z_{s_{2}}|^{2}-|Z_{s_{1}}|^{2}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{\eta}}\Biggr)^{1/2};Z_{s}\neq 0,T_{0}(Z)\circ\vartheta_{s}\leq{\varepsilon}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(f)}{{\varepsilon}^{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\Biggl[\Biggl(\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq t_{0}}}{{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}}}}\frac{||Z_{s_{2}}|^{2}-|Z_{s_{1}}|^{2}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{\eta}}\Biggr)^{2\nu}\Biggr]^{\frac{1}{4\nu}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})\right]^{\frac{4\nu-1}{4\nu}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq C(f){\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\Biggl[\Biggl(\sup_{0\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq t_{0}}\frac{||Z_{s_{2}}|^{2}-|Z_{s_{1}}|^{2}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{\eta}}\Biggr)^{2\nu}\Biggr]^{\frac{1}{4\nu}}\\
&\quad\;\times\Biggl(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})\right]\Biggr)^{\frac{4\nu-1}{4\nu}},\end{split}

where the second inequality holds since
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq{\varepsilon}
implies
|s_{2}-s_{1}|^{\frac{\eta}{2}}\leq{\varepsilon}^{\frac{\eta}{2}}
,
and the next to the last inequality applies Hölder’s inequality with respect to the pair of Hölder conjugates
(p,q)=(4\nu,\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1})
and the Markov property of
\{|Z_{t}|\}
at time
s
.
By the choice of
\eta
and
\nu
in achieving (
4.97
) and by (
4.91
), the first expectation on the right-hand side of the last inequality, independent of
{\varepsilon}>0
and
s,t
, is finite. By the last inequality, (
4.69
) holds if

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})\right]=0\quad\mbox{uniformly in $s_{0}\leq s\leq t_{0}$.}

(4.100)

To prove (
4.100
), first, note that, in the case of
Z_{s}\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})&={\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}({\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{0}(Z)\leq 1)\leq{\mathrm{e}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}[{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\varepsilon}^{-1}T_{0}(Z)}]\\
&={\mathrm{e}}\frac{K_{0}(\sqrt{2(\beta+{\varepsilon}^{-1})}|Z_{s}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)},\end{split}

(4.101)

where the inequality follows from the Markov inequality, and the last equality uses the exact formula of the Laplace transform of
T_{0}(Z)=T_{0}(|Z|)
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
. [This exact formula appears in
[
18
, (2.9), p.884]
and can be obtained from (
2.9
).] Also,

\displaystyle K_{0}(x)\lesssim{\mathrm{e}}^{-x}/\sqrt{x},\quad x\geq 1,

(4.102)

by the asymptotic representation (
1.26
) of
K_{0}(x)
as
x\to\infty
, and since the radial process
\{|Z_{t}|\}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
is a version of
{\rm BES}(0,\beta{\downarrow})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq r\leq t_{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|)}\right]<\infty,\quad\forall\;z^{0}\in\mathbb{C},

(4.103)

by using (
2.5
) since
P_{t}\mathds{1}\equiv 1
. Now, for all
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and all
{\varepsilon}>0
small such that
\sqrt{2(\beta+{\varepsilon}^{-1})}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}\geq 1
and
{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}/\sqrt{2\beta}\in(0,\delta_{\ref{ineq:BESb-2}})
, considering separately
|Z_{s}|\leq{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}
and
|Z_{s}|>{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}

and applying (
4.101
) and the decreasing monotonicity of
K_{0}
give the first inequality below:

\displaystyle\quad\;\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}(|Z_{s}|\leq{\varepsilon}^{\gamma})

\displaystyle\quad\;+\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2(\beta+{\varepsilon}^{-1})}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}){\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|)}\right]

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq\frac{C(\beta,s_{0},t_{0})({\varepsilon}^{\gamma}/\sqrt{2\beta})^{2}|\log^{3}({\varepsilon}^{\gamma}/\sqrt{2\beta})|}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}\\
&\quad+\frac{C}{{\varepsilon}^{(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1}}}\cdot\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-x}}{\sqrt{x}}\Bigg|_{x=\sqrt{2(\beta+{\varepsilon}^{-1})}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}\sup_{s_{0}\leq r\leq t_{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|)}\right]\xrightarrow[{\varepsilon}\searrow 0]{}0,\end{split}

(4.104)

where the convergence is uniform in
s_{0}\leq s\leq t_{0}
. Note that the last inequality uses Lemma
4.10
and (
4.102
), and the limit holds by applying (
4.97
) to the first term and the choice
\gamma<1/2
and (
4.103
) to the second term. The uniform convergence in (
4.104
) proves (
4.100
). The proof of Proposition
4.9
is complete.

\blacksquare

4.3.2
Differentiation in an excursion interval

Proposition 4.15.

For all
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
0<s<\infty
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}[f(Z_{{\varepsilon}})-f(Z_{0});T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}];Z_{s}\neq 0\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})],

(4.105)

where
\mathscr{A}f
is defined by (
4.13
).

We will prove Proposition
4.15
right after the following lemma, which bounds negative moments of two-dimensional standard Brownian motion.

Lemma 4.16.

For all
\eta\in[1,2)
and
z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{(0)}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta)|z^{0}|^{-\eta},\quad\forall\;r\geq 0.

(4.106)

Proof.
By the Brownian scaling property,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{(0)}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]=|z^{0}|^{-\eta}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{1}[|Z_{r/|z^{0}|^{1/2}}|^{-\eta}].

(4.107)

To bound the expectation on the right-hand side, first, note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{1}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]\lesssim r^{-\eta/2},\quad r>0,

(4.108)

where the first inequality uses the comparison theorem of SDEs
[
25
, 2.18 Proposition, p.293]
in the case of
{\rm BES}Q(0)
, and the second inequality holds by the Brownian scaling property and the property
{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{0}[|Z_{1}|^{-\eta}]<\infty
under
\eta\in[1,2)
.

We now improve (
4.108
) for
r\to 0
to an order-
1
bound
by showing

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{1}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]\leq C(\eta),\quad\forall\;0<r<1/2.

(4.109)

To see this bound, we use the PDFs of
\{|Z_{t}|\}\sim{\rm BES}(0)

[
34
, p.446]

and then the asymptotic representation
I_{0}(x)\sim 1
as
x\to 0
and
I_{0}(x)\sim{\mathrm{e}}^{x}/\sqrt{2\pi x}
as
x\to\infty

[
29
, p.136]
to get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{1}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y^{1-\eta}}{r}\exp\left(-\frac{1+y^{2}}{2r}\right)I_{0}\left(\frac{y}{r}\right){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\frac{y^{1-\eta}}{r}\exp\left(-\frac{1+y^{2}}{2r}\right){\mathrm{d}}y+\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{y^{1-\eta}}{r}\exp\left(-\frac{1+y^{2}}{2r}\right)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{y/r}}{\sqrt{y/r}}{\mathrm{d}}y.

Since
\int_{0+}y^{1-\eta}{\mathrm{d}}y<\infty
by the assumption
\eta\in[1,2)
, the first integral on the right-hand side vanishes as
r\to 0
. For the second integral when
0<r<1/2
, we write it as

\displaystyle\quad\;\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{y^{1-\eta}}{r}\exp\left(-\frac{1+y^{2}}{2r}\right)\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{y/r}}{\sqrt{y/r}}{\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{r}^{r+1/2}+\int_{r+1/2}^{\infty}\biggr)\frac{y^{1/2-\eta}}{r^{1/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-1)^{2}}{2r}\right){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=\int_{r}^{r+1/2}\frac{y^{1/2-\eta}}{r^{1/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-1)^{2}}{2r}\right){\mathrm{d}}y+\int_{\frac{r-1/2}{\sqrt{r}}}^{\infty}(\sqrt{r}\tilde{y}+1)^{1/2-\eta}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{y}^{2}}{2}\right){\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r}^{r+1/2}\frac{y^{1/2-\eta}}{r^{1/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{(r-1/2)^{2}}{2r}\right){\mathrm{d}}y+C(\eta),

(4.110)

where the second equality uses the change of variables
y=\sqrt{r}\tilde{y}+1
.
Note that the last integral bounds the integral over
y\in(r,r+1/2)
on the left-hand side of (
4.110
)
since
0<r<1/2
, and we use the assumption
\eta\in[1,2)
to bound the integral over
\tilde{y}\in[(r-1/2)/\sqrt{r},\infty)
on the left-hand side of (
4.110
) by
(\sqrt{r}\tilde{y}+1)^{1/2-\eta}\leq(r+1/2)^{1/2-\eta}
in order to get
C(\eta)
in (
4.110
).
The last integral vanishes as
r\to 0
by the fast decay of
\exp\{-(r-1/2)^{2}/(2r)\}
to zero as
r\to 0
. Hence, the last two displays imply (
4.109
).

Finally, combining (
4.108
) and (
4.109
) proves
{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{1}[|Z_{r}|^{-\eta}]\leq C(\eta)
for all
r\geq 0
. Applying this bound to the right-hand side of (
4.107
) proves the required bound
(
4.106
).

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
4.15
.

Fix
0<s<\infty
. By Remark
4.3
and Itô’s formula,

\displaystyle\quad\;\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}[f(Z_{{\varepsilon}})-f(Z_{0});T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}];Z_{s}\neq 0\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left\langle\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}W_{r}\right\rangle;T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]

\displaystyle={\rm I}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}+{\rm II}_{\ref{SDET01-1}},

(4.111)

where
\left\langle\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}W_{r}\right\rangle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\;\partial_{x}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}{\rm Re}(W_{r})+\partial_{y}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}{\rm Im}(W_{r})
with
f(x+{\mathtt{i}}y)
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, understood as
f(x,y)
in taking the partial derivatives of
f
,
and we set

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r;T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right],

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left\langle\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}W_{r}\right\rangle;T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{W}_{r}\rangle;T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right].

(4.112)

Here,

\widetilde{W}
is a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
defined as follows by using an independent two-dimensional standad Brownian motion
W^{\prime}
with
W^{\prime}_{0}=0
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{t}\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,W_{t\wedge T_{0}(Z)}+W^{\prime}_{[t-T_{0}(Z)]\vee 0},\quad t\geq 0.

Hence,
\int_{0}^{\cdot}{\langle}\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{W}_{r}{\rangle}
is a martingale under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}
, and (
4.112
) follows.

To prove (
4.105
), (
4.111
) shows that it is enough to prove the following limits:

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}{\rm I}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}={\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})],\quad\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}{\rm II}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}=0,

(4.113)

which will be done in Steps 1 and 2 below.

Step 1.
To obtain the first limit in (
4.113
), we write

\displaystyle\quad\;\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r;T_{0}(Z)>{\varepsilon}\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[\mathscr{A}f(Z_{s})]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]

(4.114)

by using (
2.29
) and the fact that
Z_{s}
has a probability density with respect to the Lebesgue measure due to (
2.5
). Also, note that by continuity,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(z^{1})=0\mbox{ ${\mathbb{P}}_{z^{1}}^{(0)}$-a.s., $\forall\;z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}$.}

(4.115)

To find the limit of the right-hand side of (
4.114
), we will prove in the remaining of Step 1 suitable integrability conditions to exchange limits and expectations in the fashion of

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}\;\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}={\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}=\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}.

(4.116)

More specifically, these integrability conditions validate a standard theorem of uniform integrability on exchanging limits and expectations (e.g.
[
5
, 6.5.2 Theorem, p.263]
).

To justify the first equality of (
4.116
) in the context of (
4.114
), we show that

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)},\quad{\varepsilon}\in(0,1),\\
\displaystyle\mbox{is uniformly integrable under ${\mathbb{P}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}$, $\forall\;z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}$}.\end{array}\right.

To see (
4.3.2
), fix
z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
, and note that for all
1<p<2
, pairs of Hölder conjugates
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
for
1<p^{\prime}<\infty
such that
1<pp^{\prime}<2
, and
\nu\in[1,2)
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}\right|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\biggr)^{pp^{\prime}}\biggr]^{1/p^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}\biggr)^{pq^{\prime}}\biggr]^{1/q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}[|\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r})|^{pp^{\prime}}]{\mathrm{d}}r\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}\frac{1}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|z^{1}|)^{p}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}[K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)^{pq^{\prime}}]^{1/q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,z^{1},p,p^{\prime},f,\nu)\left(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\{1+{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}[|Z_{r}|^{-pp^{\prime}}]\}{\mathrm{d}}r\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}[|Z_{\varepsilon}|^{-\nu}+1]^{1/q^{\prime}}.

(4.119)

Note that the last inequality holds by using the following four properties: (i) the bound

\displaystyle|\mathscr{A}f(z^{1})|\leq C(f)+C(f)/|z^{1}|

(4.120)

due to (
4.13
) and the asymptotic representations (
1.25
)–(
1.28
) of
K_{0}
and
K_{1}
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
; (ii) the inequality
(x+y)^{pp^{\prime}}\leq C(pp^{\prime})(x^{pp^{\prime}}+y^{pp^{\prime}})
for all
x,y\geq 0
; (iii) the asymptotic representations (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) of
K_{0}
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
; (iv) the fact that
|\log x^{-1}|^{pq^{\prime}}\leq C(q^{\prime},\nu)x^{-\nu}
for all
0<x\leq 1
. Applying Lemma
4.16
to (
4.119
) yields

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{z^{1}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}\right|^{p}\right]<\infty,\quad\forall\;z^{1}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\},

which is enough to get (
4.3.2
) since
1<p<2
by assumption.

Next, to justify the second equality of (
4.116
) in the context of (
4.114
), we show the following property:

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{s}}^{(0)}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right],\quad\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,1),\\
\\
\mbox{is uniformly integrable under }{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}},\quad\forall\;z^{0}\in\mathbb{C}.\end{array}\right.

To this end, note that for all
{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{s}}^{(0)}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{s}}^{(0)}\left[|\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r})|\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta r}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{r}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}\right]{\mathrm{d}}r+C(f)\left(1+\frac{1}{|Z_{s}|}\right).

(4.124)

Here, we have used (
2.29
) again to get the first term on the right-hand side, and the last term uses (
4.120
).
To bound the ratio of
K_{0}
’s on the right-hand side of (
4.124
), we use the asymptotic representations (
1.25
)–(
1.26
) of
K_{0}
as
x\to 0
and
x\to\infty
to get
K_{0}(x)\lesssim x^{-\nu}
for any
\nu\in(0,1)
as
x\to\ 0
and

K_{0}(x)\gtrsim{\mathrm{e}}^{-2x}
as
x\to\infty
. Hence,
(
4.124
) gives, when
Z_{s}\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{s}}^{(0)}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,f){\mathrm{e}}^{2\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|}\cdot\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{s}}^{(0)}\left[\frac{1}{|Z_{r}|^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{|Z_{r}|^{1+\nu}}\right]{\mathrm{d}}r+C(f)\left(1+\frac{1}{|Z_{s}|}\right)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq C(\beta,f,\nu){\mathrm{e}}^{2\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{s}|}\left(\frac{1}{|Z_{s}|^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{|Z_{s}|^{1+\nu}}\right)\\
&\quad\;+C(f)\left(1+\frac{1}{|Z_{s}|}\right)\in L^{1+\eta}({\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{z^{0}}),\quad\forall\;\nu\in(0,1),\;{\varepsilon}\in(0,1),\end{split}

(4.125)

where
\eta=\eta(\nu)
is some number in
(0,1)
, the inequality in (
4.125
) follows from Lemma
4.16
, and the
L^{1+\eta}
-integrability in (
4.125
) can be justified by applying Hölder’s inequality to the first term and then applying
(
4.19
) and the bound
{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{1}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[{\mathrm{e}}^{a|Z_{s}|}]<\infty
for any
a>0
due to Lemma
4.12
. By (
4.125
), we obtain (
4.3.2
).

The limit of the right-hand side of (
4.114
) can now be evaluated as follows. By using (
4.115
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
) in the same order, we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}\left[\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}\left(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right)\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\varepsilon}\searrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}^{(0)}_{Z_{s}}\left[\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{A}f(Z_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-\beta{\varepsilon}}K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{\varepsilon}|)}{K_{0}(\sqrt{2\beta}|Z_{0}|)}-\mathscr{A}f(Z_{0})\right];Z_{s}\neq 0\right].

By (
4.114
), the last equality is enough to get the first limit in (
4.113
).

Step 2.

It remains to prove the second limit of (
4.113
). Let
(p,q)
be a pair of Hölder conjugates
(p,q)
with
1<p<\infty
to be chosen. By Hölder’s inequality with respect to this pair
(p,q)
, we obtain the first inequality below:

\displaystyle|{\rm II}_{\ref{SDET01-1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\biggl[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}{\langle}\nabla f(Z_{r}),{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{W}_{r}{\rangle}\biggr|^{p}\biggr]^{1/p}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})^{1/q}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(p)}{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}\biggl[{\mathbb{E}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}\biggl[(C(f){\varepsilon})^{p/2}\biggr]^{1/p}{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})^{1/q}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(p,f)}{{\varepsilon}^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})^{1/q}]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,f)\left(\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}^{q/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{z^{0}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}[{\mathbb{P}}^{\beta{\downarrow}}_{Z_{s}}(T_{0}(Z)\leq{\varepsilon})]\right)^{1/q},

(4.126)

where the second inequality uses the inequality
(x+y)^{p/2}\leq C(p)(x^{p/2}+y^{p/2})
for all
x,y\geq 0
and
1<p<\infty

and the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
34
, (4.1) Theorem, p.160]
, and the last inequality applies Hölder’s inequality again.

Now, we choose the pair
(p,q)
such that for some
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,1/2)
,

2\gamma^{\prime}>q/2
. Since
s>0
, it follows from a straightforward modification of the derivation of (
4.104
) with
(\gamma,(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\frac{4\nu}{4\nu-1})
in (
4.104
) replaced by
(\gamma^{\prime},q/2)

that the right-hand side of (
4.126
) tends to zero as
{\varepsilon}\searrow 0
. The proof of Proposition
4.15
is complete.

\blacksquare

5
Transformations to skew-product diffusions

In this section, we comprehensively study transformations to skew-product diffusions by specifying the radial and angular parts. The following assumption generalizes the setting for (
1.10
).

Assumption 5.1 (Radial part and angular part).

Given
\alpha_{\varrho}\in[0,1/2)
and
\varrho_{0}>0
, we assume the existence of a process
\{\varrho_{t}\}
satisfying the following SDE for all
0\leq t<\infty
:

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}=\varrho_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1-2\alpha_{\varrho}}{2\varrho_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+A_{\varrho}(t)+W_{\varrho}(t),

(5.1)

such that
\varrho_{t}\geq 0
and
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/\varrho_{s}<\infty
, where
\{A_{\varrho}(t)\}
is a real-valued, adapted continuous process of finite variation with
A_{\varrho}(0)=0
, and
\{W_{\varrho}(t)\}
is a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion with
W_{\varrho}(0)=0
. Here and in what follows, a process of
finite variation
is one such that the total variation on any compact interval is finite with probability one.

Given
\alpha_{\vartheta}\in[0,1/2)
and
\vartheta_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, let
\{\vartheta_{t};t<T_{0}(\varrho)\}
be given by

\displaystyle\vartheta_{t}=\vartheta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}}{\varrho_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s),\quad t<T_{0}(\varrho),

(5.2)

for a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{W_{\vartheta}(t)\}{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\{W_{\varrho}(t)\}
with
W_{\vartheta}(0)=0
, where
T_{0}(\varrho)
is defined as in (
1.11
).
∎

The key role of Assumption
5.1
is played by the radial proces
\{\varrho_{t}\}
, since
the process
\{\vartheta_{t};t<T_{0}(\varrho)\}
can be
constructed
according to (
5.2
) as soon as
\{\varrho_{t}\}
and
\{W_{\vartheta}(t)\}
are given. Also,
by the Dambis–Dubins–Schwarz theorem
[
34
, (1.6) Theorem on p.181]
, (
5.2
) implies

\vartheta_{t}=\gamma_{\int_{0}^{t}(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}){\mathrm{d}}s/\varrho_{s}^{2}},\quad t<T_{0}(\varrho),

for a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{\gamma_{t}\}
. Recall the angular process in (
1.10
). Our motivation of considering also
\alpha_{\vartheta}\in(0,1/2)
is given in Example
5.2
(1
∘
) below, although such a choice of
\alpha_{\vartheta}
is not used in the other sections of this paper.

Example 5.2.

(1
∘
) Set
A_{\varrho}(t)=0
and
\alpha_{\vartheta}=\alpha_{\varrho}\in(0,1/2)
. In this case, the generator of the continuous extension, in the sense of Erickson
[
19
]
, for the skew-product diffusion
\{\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}};t<T_{0}(\varrho)\}
has been specified in
[
19
, (8.2) of §8 (a)]
,
since
\{\varrho_{t}\}
in (
5.1
) is a Bessel process of index
-\alpha_{\varrho}
, or equivalently, of dimension
2-2\alpha_{\varrho}
.

(2
∘
) In the case of
{\rm BES}(0,\beta{\downarrow})
, the SDE of
\{\varrho_{t}\}
is given by (
1.12
) with
\rho_{t}=\varrho_{t}
. The equivalent under (
5.1
) is the one with the following choice:

\alpha_{\varrho}=0
and
A_{\varrho}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}(-\sqrt{2\beta})(K_{1}/K_{0})(\sqrt{2\beta}\varrho_{s}){\mathrm{d}}s
. ∎

The next proposition is the main result of Section
5
. We work with the following complex-valued process defined under Assumption
5.1
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(t)&=U_{\mathcal{Z}}(t)+{\mathtt{i}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(t)\\
&\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{[\cos\vartheta_{s}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)-\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}\sin\vartheta_{s}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)]}{\sqrt{\cos^{2}\vartheta_{s}+(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})\sin^{2}\vartheta_{s}}}\\
&\quad\;\;+{\mathtt{i}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{[\sin\vartheta_{s}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)+\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}\cos\vartheta_{s}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)]}{\sqrt{\sin^{2}\vartheta_{s}+(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})\cos^{2}\vartheta_{s}}},\quad 0\leq t<T_{0}(\varrho).\end{split}

(5.3)

Note that
\{W_{\mathcal{Z}}(t);0\leq t<T_{0}(\varrho)\}
extends to a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{W_{\mathcal{Z}}(t);0\leq t<\infty\}
by joining it with an independent copy of two-dimensional standard Brownian motion with zero initial condition at time
T_{0}(\varrho)
and using Lévy’s characterization of Brownian motion
[
34
, (3.6) Theorem, p.150]
.

Proposition 5.3.

(1
∘
)
Under Assumption
5.1
,
\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\mathcal{X}_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\mathcal{Y}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}
,
t<T_{0}(\varrho)
, satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{Z}_{t}&=\mathcal{Z}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{(1-2\alpha_{\varrho})-(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})}{2\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\frac{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}{\overline{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}}{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)\right)\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\cos^{2}\vartheta_{s}+(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})\sin^{2}\vartheta_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathtt{i}}\sqrt{\sin^{2}\vartheta_{s}+(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})\cos^{2}\vartheta_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s).\end{split}

(5.4)

(2
∘
)
Conversely, suppose that
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
and
\{\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\mathcal{X}_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\mathcal{Y}_{t};t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})\}
is a complex-valued process satisfying
\mathcal{Z}_{0}\neq 0
and (
5.4
). Let
\vartheta_{0}
be any constant chosen to satisfy
\mathcal{Z}_{0}/|\mathcal{Z}_{0}|={\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{0}}
. Then for
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
,
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
can be decomposed as
\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}
for

\varrho_{t}=|\mathcal{Z}_{t}|
obeying (
5.1
) and
\vartheta_{t}
obeying (
5.2
), with
T_{0}(\varrho)=T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{\varrho}(t)&=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{X}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)+\mathcal{Y}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\\
W_{\vartheta}(t)&=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{-\mathcal{Y}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)+\mathcal{X}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}.\end{split}

(5.5)

Moreover, if
\{\mathcal{Z}_{t};t\geq 0\}
satisfies (
5.4
) and
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|<\infty
for all
0\leq t<\infty
, then
\varrho_{t}=|\mathcal{Z}_{t}|
obeys the SDE in (
5.1
) for all
0\leq t<\infty
.

Remark 5.4.

If
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|<\infty
, we must have
\int_{0}^{t}\mathds{1}_{\{\mathcal{Z}_{s}=0\}}{\mathrm{d}}s=0
. Hence, as in the case of (
5.3
) for
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
, the processes in (
5.5
) can be extended to independent one-dimensional standard Brownian motions over
0\leq t<\infty
.

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
5.3
(1
∘
).

It follows from (
5.2
) and Itô’s formula that for
t<T_{0}(\varrho)
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathtt{i}}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}}{\varrho_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}(-{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}})\frac{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}{\varrho_{s}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s.

(5.6)

Since
\{W_{\varrho}(t)\}{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\{W_{\vartheta}(t)\}
by assumption, (
5.1
) and integration by parts give, for
t<T_{0}(\varrho)
,

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}

\displaystyle=\varrho_{0}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}\varrho_{s}\left(-\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})}{2\varrho_{s}^{2}}\right){\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\varrho_{s}\left(\frac{{\mathtt{i}}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}}{\varrho_{s}}\right){\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}\left(\frac{1-2\alpha_{\varrho}}{2\varrho_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)\right)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\varrho_{0}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{(1-2\alpha_{\varrho})-(1-2\alpha_{\vartheta})}{2\varrho_{s}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}}{\mathrm{d}}s+{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)\right)\\
&\quad\;+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{s}}[{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)+{\mathtt{i}}\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)].\end{split}

(5.7)

To see that the last term equals the sum of the last two terms in (
5.4
), note that

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}[{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(t)+{\mathtt{i}}\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(t)]

\displaystyle=(\cos\vartheta_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\sin\vartheta_{t})[{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(t)+{\mathtt{i}}\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(t)]

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=[\cos\vartheta_{t}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(t)-\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}\sin\vartheta_{t}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(t)]\\
&\quad\;+{\mathtt{i}}[\sin\vartheta_{t}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(t)+\sqrt{1-2\alpha_{\vartheta}}\cos\vartheta_{t}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(t)].\end{split}

(5.8)

We obtain (
5.4
) by combining (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) and using the definition (
5.3
) of
\{W_{\mathcal{Z}}(t)\}
.

\blacksquare

The following lemma prepares the proof of Proposition
5.3
(2
∘
) and has been applied independently earlier. The statement writes
\int_{0}^{t}f(s)|{\mathrm{d}}A|_{s}
for the Lebesgue–Stieltjes integral such that the integrator is the total variation of a process
\{A_{t}\}
of finite variation.

Lemma 5.5.

Fix
\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
.
Let
\tau
be a finite stopping time,
\{A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{t};0\leq t\leq\tau\}
,
\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
, be real-valued continuous adapted processes of finite variation, and
\{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{t}=\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{k}}_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{t};0\leq t\leq\tau\}
,
\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
, be complex-valued continuous adapted processes such that

\int_{0}^{\tau}|{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}|_{s})/|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|<\infty
. Assume that for a two-dimensional standard Brownian motion
W^{\mathbf{j}}=U^{\mathbf{j}}+{\mathtt{i}}V^{\mathbf{j}}
with
W^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}=0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}=\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}}{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}+W^{\mathbf{j}}_{t},\quad 0\leq t\leq\tau.

(5.9)

Then for all
0\leq t\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|^{2}+2t+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}2{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}2(\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}),

(5.10)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{2|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}.

(5.11)

Here, (
5.11
) holds to the degree that
\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}s/|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|<\infty
for all
0\leq t\leq\tau
.

Proof.
We prove (
5.10
) first.
By the assumption that
\{A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{t}\}
are real-valued, taking complex conjugates of both sides of (
5.9
) gives

\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}=\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}/\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}+\overline{W}_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}
.
Hence, by integration by parts and the identities
|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2}=\mathcal{Z}_{t}^{\mathbf{j}}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}
and
{\langle}W^{\mathbf{j}},\overline{W}^{\mathbf{j}}\rangle_{t}=2t
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2}&=|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|^{2}+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\frac{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}({\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+{\mathtt{i}}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s})\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Z}_{s}^{\mathbf{j}}\overline{({\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+{\mathtt{i}}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s})}+2t.\end{split}

(5.12)

To complete the proof of (
5.10
), it is enough to write
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}=\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+{\mathtt{i}}\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}
, and note that for any complex numbers
z^{1}=x+{\mathtt{i}}y
and
z^{2}=u+{\mathtt{i}}v
,

\overline{z^{1}}z^{2}+z^{1}\overline{z^{2}}=2xu+2yv
.

To get (
5.11
), we first apply Itô’s formula to
f(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2})
,
0\leq t\leq\tau
, with
f(x)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(x+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}
,
x\geq 0
, for fixed
{\varepsilon}>0
. Since
f^{\prime}(x)=1/[2(x+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}]
and
f^{\prime\prime}(x)=-1/[4(x+{\varepsilon})^{3/2}]
, (
5.10
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\;(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}\\
&=(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}\biggl[1-\frac{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}}{2(|Z_{s}^{\mathbf{j}}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\quad\;+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}.\end{split}

(5.13)

Next, to obtain the precise limits of both sides of (
5.13
) later, we show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}<\infty,\quad\forall\;0\leq t\leq\tau.

(5.14)

To see (
5.14
), note that the integrand of
{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}
on the right-hand side of (
5.13
) is bounded by
1/|\mathcal{Z}^{k}_{s}|
, and
1-a/[2(a+{\varepsilon})]\geq 1/2
for all
a\geq 0
. Hence, by rearrangement and then Fatou’s lemma, (
5.13
) implies the following bound for any given sequence
{\varepsilon}_{n}\searrow 0
:

\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{2|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}\leq|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{t}|+|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{0}|+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}|_{s}}{|Z^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|}+\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon}_{n})^{1/2}}\right|.

Moreover, for
0<T<\infty
, we can choose such a sequence
\{{\varepsilon}_{n}\}
such that
the right-hand side is finite for all
0\leq t\leq T\wedge\tau
. Specifically, the third term on the right-hand side is finite by the assumption
\int_{0}^{\tau}|{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}|_{s})/|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|<\infty
for all
\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
, and the choice of
\{{\varepsilon}_{n}\}
handles the fourth term by the dominated convergence theorem for stochastic integrals
[
33
, Theorem 32, p.176]
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau}\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}-\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau}\mathds{1}_{\{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|>0\}}\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}+\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}\right|=0,

(5.15)

where the limit is in the sense of convergence in probability. We have proved (
5.14
).

We are ready to complete the proof of (
5.11
).
By the usual dominated convergence theorem, the bound in (
5.14
) and the assumption
\int_{0}^{\tau}|{\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}|_{s}/|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}|<\infty

give, for all
0\leq t\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}\biggl[1-\frac{|Z^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}}{2(|Z_{s}^{\mathbf{j}}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{2|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|^{2}+{\varepsilon})^{1/2}}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}|}{\rm Re}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}_{s}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{s}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}_{s}.

(5.17)

The required identity in (
5.11
) follows by applying (
5.14
), (
5.15
), (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) to (
5.13
).

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
5.3
(2
∘
).

First, the SDE of
\varrho_{t}=|\mathcal{Z}_{t}|
,
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, follows by applying (
5.10
) with the following choice for a fixed
\mathbf{j}
:
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{j}}=\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{X}+{\mathtt{i}}\mathcal{Y}
,
W^{\mathbf{j}}=W_{\mathcal{Z}}=U_{\mathcal{Z}}+{\mathtt{i}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}
,
A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{j}}_{t}=-\alpha_{\varrho}t+\int_{0}^{t}|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)
,
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}=A^{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}}=0
for all
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{j}
. Then
(
5.11
) becomes

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}

\displaystyle=\varrho_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{2\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{[-\alpha_{\varrho}{\mathrm{d}}s+\varrho_{s}{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)]}{\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{X}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)+\mathcal{Y}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},

which simplifies to (
5.1
) with
W_{\varrho}(t)
,
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, defined in (
5.5
). We have proved the required SDE (
5.1
) for
\{\varrho_{t};t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})\}
in the case of
\{\mathcal{Z}_{t};t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})\}
. The same argument applies to the case of
\{\mathcal{Z}_{t};t\geq 0\}
, so the required SDE (
5.1
) for
\{\varrho_{t};t\geq 0\}
holds.

Next, we show the required identity
\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}
,
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, of Proposition
5.3
(2
∘
) for
\varrho_{t}
obeying (
5.1
) and
\vartheta_{t}
obeying (
5.2
) with
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
,
W_{\vartheta}
defined in (
5.5
),
T_{0}(\varrho)=T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, and
\vartheta_{0}
such that
\mathcal{Z}_{0}=\varrho_{0}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{0}}
. Note that
\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\varrho_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}}

for all
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
is equivalent to
\mathcal{Z}_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{t}}=1
for all
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, where
H_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\log\varrho_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}
.

We verify
\mathcal{Z}_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{t}}=1
for all
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
by Itô’s formula. First, for
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
, (
5.1
) gives

\displaystyle\log\varrho_{t}

\displaystyle=\log\varrho_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1-2\alpha_{\varrho}}{2\varrho_{s}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\varrho_{s}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\log\varrho_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\alpha_{\varrho}}{\varrho^{2}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}.

By the last equality and (
5.2
) for
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
, the SDE of
H_{t}=\log\varrho_{t}+{\mathtt{i}}\vartheta_{t}
for
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
is

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle=H_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\alpha_{\varrho}}{\varrho^{2}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}+{\mathtt{i}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\vartheta}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}

\displaystyle=H_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\alpha_{\varrho}}{\varrho^{2}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}.

(5.18)

Here, the last term of (
5.18
) follows by using (
5.5
) and the computation that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{X}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)+\mathcal{Y}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}}+{\mathtt{i}}\frac{-\mathcal{Y}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}U_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)+\mathcal{X}_{s}{\mathrm{d}}V_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{s}}{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)=\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}.

Next, by Itô’s formula, we obtain from (
5.18
) and the identity
{\langle}W_{\mathcal{Z}},W_{\mathcal{Z}}{\rangle}_{t}\equiv 0
that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{t}}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{\alpha_{\varrho}}{\varrho_{s}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}-\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{\mathcal{Z}_{s}},\quad t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z}).

Hence, by integration by parts, (
5.4
) with
\alpha_{\vartheta}=0
, and the identity
{\langle}W_{\mathcal{Z}},W_{\mathcal{Z}}{\rangle}_{t}\equiv 0
, for all
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{t}}

\displaystyle=1+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{-\alpha_{\varrho}}{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}{\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Z}_{s}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{\alpha_{\varrho}}{\varrho_{s}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Z}_{s}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}A_{\varrho}(s)}{\varrho_{s}}-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Z}_{s}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{s}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}W_{\mathcal{Z}}(s)}{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}=1,

which is the required identity
\mathcal{Z}_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-H_{t}}=1
for
t<T_{0}(\mathcal{Z})
. The proof is complete.

\blacksquare
